Chapter 6

Business Requirements Document

Source: docs/gl-ap-ar/01-business-requirements-document.md

Note

Generated by scripts/build-gl-docs.mjs from docs/gl-ap-ar/source. Do not edit; change the source and rebuild.

Product Warraq ERP 2.0 — finance core (General Ledger, Receivables, Payables, Bank)
Version / date 1.0 / 2026-10-15
Status Baseline for the delivered scope (Sprints 0–9); planned scope in Sprints 11–20
Source documents docs/reference/Finance-Core-Blueprint.pdf (v0.1), owner decisions recorded in docs/archive/gl-step1-plan.md, docs/design-notes/gl-step1-5-decisions.md, docs/design-notes/ar-ap-bank-design.md, docs/design-notes/date-fields-standard.md, docs/design-notes/posting-control.md
Related SRS (02), Solution design (03), Technical design (04), Test strategy (05), User manual (06), Sprint log (07), Traceability (08)

1. Purpose

Replace document-derived bookkeeping with a real double-entry ledger that sales, purchasing, inventory and (later) payroll all settle into, so that accounts receivable, accounts payable, bank, tax and every financial statement are queries over the same posted lines. This document states, once and traceably, what the business needs; the SRS states what the system shall do about it.

2. Business context and problem

Before 2.0 the system computed every report from documents (invoices, expenses, bills, vouchers). That is correct for a single entity but gives accountants no chart of accounts of their own, no manual adjustments, no analytical dimensions, no subledger tied to a control account, no multi-document receipts, no bank reconciliation and no period control beyond month close. Tier-1 ERP practice (SAP, Oracle, Dynamics) treats all of these as basics.

3. Objectives

  1. One ledger, one posting engine, one report engine: no figure is calculated in two places.
  2. The accountant can see, adjust, analyse and close the books without developer help.
  3. Receivables and payables behave as subledgers: every party balance ties to its control account to the cent.
  4. Money movements (receipts, payments, bank charges) are controlled, auditable and reconcilable to the bank.
  5. Nothing can be posted outside the period rules, and every override is attributable.
  6. The solution serves thousands of concurrent users across many companies, safely isolated.

4. Scope

In scope: 178 business requirements in 27 areas (section 6). 114 are built, 5 partly built, 59 planned.

Out of scope (this programme):

  • Fixed assets and depreciation (classes, CWIP, methods, runs, disposal, transfer, documents) (FR-FA-01..10) — A later phase; the ledger and dimensions are designed not to block it.
  • Landed cost / import costing (FR-LC-01..05) — A later phase tied to procurement.
  • Manufacturing and project work-in-progress costing (FR-WIP-01..08) — No production module yet.
  • Multi-currency, intercompany, payroll calculation ((Blueprint scope)) — Declared out of scope by the Blueprint for this phase.
  • Bank feeds / open-banking integration ((Decision)) — Not requested; statement lines are typed or imported.

5. Stakeholders and users

Role Interest
Owner / CFO Reliable statements, period control, approval safeguards, audit
Accountant (preparer) Chart, journals, reconciliation, receipts and payments
Approver / Finance manager Approves journals, reopens reconciliations, overrides locks
AR / AP clerk Receipts, payments, aging, statements
Branch manager Branch-limited views
Auditor Read access, audit trail, immutable postings
Administrator Roles, permissions, company setup
Developer / support Documentation, tests, release safety

6. Business requirements register

Status: Built = delivered in Demo 2.0; Partial = delivered in part (gap named); Planned = in scope, not built. Priority: MoSCoW. "Blueprint" = Finance Core Blueprint requirement ids. Sprints are listed in the sprint log (07).

COA — Chart of accounts and account mapping

BR-COA-01 Company-owned hierarchical chart — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-COA-01, source Blueprint, sprint S1. Every company has its own chart of accounts: code, English and Arabic name, type (asset, liability, equity, revenue, expense), parent heading and active flag. Headings group and total but take no postings.

BR-COA-02 Starter chart at provisioning — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-COA-03, source Blueprint, sprint S1. A new company receives a standard Saudi chart automatically, with every posting role (receivable, payable, inventory, VAT, bank, revenue, cost of sales ...) mapped to exactly one account and its own account for each bank.

BR-COA-03 Control accounts — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-COA-02, source Blueprint, sprint S1. Accounts for customers, vendors, stock, tax and banks are control accounts: only the document or subledger that owns them posts there, and manual journals refuse them.

BR-COA-04 Retire, never delete — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-COA-04, source Blueprint, sprint S1. An account with postings can never be deleted. It can be deactivated only when its balance is zero and nothing depends on it; its type, role and bank link cannot change once used.

BR-COA-05 Show dependants before deactivation — Should, Partial, Blueprint FR-COA-05, source Blueprint, sprint S20. Before an account is deactivated the user sees every configuration record that points at it (role mappings, banks, categories, customer and vendor profiles).

BR-COA-06 Account mapping — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. The accountant chooses which account each posting role and each expense category posts to. A change applies to future postings only; old lines keep the account they were posted to.

BR-COA-07 Chart import and export — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. The chart can be exported to Excel and imported from Excel with a preview; only the rows the user selects are saved and an existing account type is never changed.

BR-COA-08 Audit of chart changes — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S1. Creating, changing, importing, mapping and deactivating accounts is written to the audit trail.

BR-COA-09 Account classification — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF4. Every account carries a class and sub-class (cash, bank, receivable, other current asset, non-current asset, current and non-current liability, equity, operating revenue, other income, cost of sales, operating expense, finance cost, tax), a nature or function for expenses, a cash-flow class and a statement mapping key, so that statutory and management statements and the cash-flow statement derive from the chart.

BR-COA-10 Posting controls and account groups — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF4. An account can be blocked for posting (distinct from inactive), restricted to automatic posting, required to carry a currency or a tax category, and belongs to an account group that supplies defaults and number ranges.

BR-COA-11 Industry chart templates — Should, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF4. A company chooses a starter chart for retail, trading, manufacturing, real estate or services, each with its role accounts, dimension rules and statement layouts, reviewed by an accountant; a template can be merged into an existing chart.

BR-COA-12 Chart views and statutory mapping — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF4. One ledger can be presented through several chart views (IFRS as adopted in Saudi Arabia as the reference, local statutory, management) that map accounts to the view lines; statements and the cash-flow statement use the selected view.

BR-COA-13 Effective dating and approval of chart changes — Should, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF4. Accounts carry valid-from and valid-to dates; changes to the chart can require approval and are audited; history is never rewritten.

JNL — Journals and opening balances

BR-JNL-01 Manual journals with maker-checker — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-01, source Decision, sprint S1, S11. An accountant prepares a journal as a draft, submits it, and a different person with the posting right approves it (which posts it) or rejects it with a written reason, returning it to draft.

BR-JNL-02 Balanced entries only — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-01, source Blueprint, sprint S1. A journal entry whose debits do not equal its credits is refused by the server inside the same transaction as the write, for documents and for manual journals alike.

BR-JNL-03 Posted entries are immutable — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-02, source Blueprint, sprint S1. A posted entry is never updated or deleted. A correction is a reversing entry plus a new correct entry; a reversal can be made only once.

BR-JNL-04 Source and dimension recorded — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-03, source Blueprint, sprint S1. Every entry records its source (document type and id) and every line carries the dimension tags and party its account requires.

BR-JNL-05 Entry numbering — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-04, source Blueprint, sprint S1. Manual journals receive a unique sequential number per company; a discarded draft keeps its number on record.

BR-JNL-06 Reversal of a manual journal — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. A posted manual journal is reversed by a reversing draft that follows the same approval path; the original stays posted until the reversal is approved. Entries made by documents are reversed through their document, never by hand.

BR-JNL-07 Self-approval safeguard — Should, Built, Blueprint FR-APR-03, source Decision, sprint S1, S11. A preparer cannot approve their own journal unless the company owner switches that safeguard on; when on, such journals are marked and the change is audited.

BR-JNL-08 Journal listing and entry detail — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. Every posting of the company, from documents and from manual journals, can be listed and filtered (period, status, source, text) and opened to its lines and its source document.

BR-JNL-09 Opening balances — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. A company that starts using the ledger part-way can post an opening entry: previewed first, balanced (a difference may be put to an equity account), posted once, with a confirmation, and reversible with a reason.

BR-JNL-10 Amount-threshold approval rules — Should, Partial, Blueprint FR-APR-01, FR-APR-02, FR-APR-03, FR-APR-04, FR-APR-05, FR-APR-06, source Blueprint, sprint S11, S11. Approval rules per document type (journal, vendor bill, vendor payment, credit note) by amount, with several sequential levels, server-enforced, with no self-approval at any level and a full approval history; a document without a matching rule posts directly. Delivered so far (Sprint 11): vendor payments, vendor bills and credit notes, recall by the submitter, delegation with validity dates, the manager hierarchy, and the "paid at delivery" goods-receipt shortcut cannot bypass a rule. Manual journals follow a rule too when one matches their amount; with no matching rule they keep the single maker-checker step.

PER — Periods, posting control and close

BR-PER-01 Open fiscal month rule — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S5. A dated posting must fall in an existing, open fiscal month and not in the future.

BR-PER-02 VAT quarter freeze — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint S5. A tax document may not be dated in a VAT quarter whose return is generated or filed; any other posting may not be dated in a filed quarter.

BR-PER-03 Lock date — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S5. The company sets a lock date; nothing may be dated on or before it except by a user holding the override right.

BR-PER-04 Back-date window — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S5. The company sets how many days back a posting may be dated; going further needs the back-date right.

BR-PER-05 Reason for back-dating — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S5. A posting older than a set number of days must carry a reason; a posting that used an override or a reason is written to the audit log.

BR-PER-06 One guard for every dated posting — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S5. Every route that posts something dated, or reverses something at its own date, passes through one posting guard, so no path can bypass the period rules. A manual journal is checked at submit and again at approval.

BR-PER-07 Month close from posted lines — Should, Partial, Blueprint FR-CLOSE-01, FR-CLOSE-02, source Blueprint, sprint S12. Closing a fiscal month verifies the month trial balance from posted journal lines, locks the period and never zeroes revenue or expense accounts; the cached figure is a performance aid, never the only source.

BR-PER-08 Month-close readiness checklist — Should, Planned, Blueprint FR-CLOSE-03, source Blueprint, sprint S12. Before a month closes the system shows one checklist: bank reconciliations completed, VAT quarters filed, no draft or pending documents dated in the month, and the other period prerequisites.

BR-PER-09 Restricted month and adjustment period — Should, Planned, Blueprint FR-CLOSE-07, FR-CLOSE-08, source Blueprint, sprint S12. A month status between open and closed that only named users may post into, and a restricted adjustment period after year-end for controller / auditor correcting entries; a closed year is never reopened.

BR-PER-10 Year-end close — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-CLOSE-04, FR-CLOSE-05, FR-CLOSE-06, source Blueprint, sprint S13. Year-end close computes the year result from posted lines and posts exactly one closing entry into retained earnings, then opens the next year automatically; balance sheet accounts carry forward with no posting.

BR-PER-11 Per-module close — Could, Planned, source Standard, sprint S12. Receivables, payables, inventory and the general ledger can each be closed on their own date, as in tier-1 systems.

DAT — Document date standard

BR-DAT-01 Document date on every document — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S4. Every document table has one field named doc_date holding the date of the document; operational reports use it.

BR-DAT-02 Posting date for the ledger — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S4. Every document that posts also has posting_date (defaulting to doc_date); financial statements, period locks and VAT use it.

BR-DAT-03 Consistent date naming — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S4. Other dates are named _date and stored as real dates, not timestamps; one concept has one name across tables, code and API.

BR-DAT-04 Audit stamps — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S4. Every document records created_at / created_by and, once edited, updated_at / updated_by.

DIM — Dimensions and cost centres

BR-DIM-01 Configurable dimension types — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-DIM-01, source Blueprint, sprint S2. The company defines any number of dimension types (Branch and Department are seeded and cannot be removed), each combinable on any ledger line.

BR-DIM-02 Dimension values and cost centres — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-DIM-02, source Blueprint, sprint S2. Dimension values form a hierarchy; cost centres (the Department dimension) have an optional home branch.

BR-DIM-03 Account dimension rules — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-JE-03, source Decision, sprint S2. Each account marks a dimension required, optional or not allowed, with an optional default; rules can be applied in bulk to a type, heading, range or list after a preview.

BR-DIM-04 Default tags from context — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. The posting engine fills dimension tags from, in order, the line, the document, the customer or vendor default, the bank default and the account default, and drops tags an account does not allow.

BR-DIM-05 Dimension filters — Must, Built, source Blueprint, sprint S2. The general ledger, journal listing and every report can be filtered by dimension, including all descendants of a hierarchical value.

BR-DIM-06 Dimensions captured on documents — Should, Planned, source Decision, sprint S16. Invoice, expense and bill forms collect dimensions, so a Required rule can be enforced on documents as it is on manual journals.

BR-DIM-07 Monthly allocation run — Could, Planned, Blueprint FR-DIM-03, source Blueprint, sprint S15. A configurable monthly run redistributes a shared cost centre across destination branches by fixed or revenue percentage into an allocated-overhead account, net zero at company level.

BR-DIM-08 Branch profit and loss subtotals — Could, Planned, Blueprint FR-DIM-04, source Blueprint, sprint S15. Branch P&L shows direct expenses and allocated overhead as separate subtotals (contribution margin, then fully loaded profit).

BR-DIM-09 Dimension type roles — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF6. A dimension type can be marked balancing (a profit unit), cost object, or statistical driver; the engine treats each role accordingly.

BR-DIM-10 Dimension value attributes and lifecycle — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF6. A dimension value carries configurable attributes (text, number, date, status, link to a customer, employee or branch) and a lifecycle (status and validity dates); posting to a value outside its validity is refused.

BR-DIM-11 Valid-combination rules between dimension types — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF6. A company can require, imply or default one dimension from another (a project belongs to one profit centre); invalid combinations are refused.

BR-DIM-12 Balancing per dimension value — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF6. For a balancing type each entry balances per value: where it does not, the engine adds inter-unit clearing lines; balance sheet and profit and loss can be produced per value. Allocation (BR-DIM-07) is widened to any dimension type, any basis including statistical drivers, with allocation cycles.

BR-DIM-13 Break-down reports — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF6. Any report can break down by any dimension type, one column or row per value rolled up through the hierarchy, with drill to the ledger.

RPT — Ledger reporting

BR-RPT-01 General ledger detail — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. For any account or set of accounts and a period, the general ledger shows opening balance, every movement with running balance, and links each line to its source document.

BR-RPT-02 One report engine — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. Trial balance, income statement, balance sheet, cash flow and changes in equity are definitions (rows by columns) run by one engine over the same ledger lines; no second calculation exists.

BR-RPT-03 Custom report designer — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. An authorised user copies a standard definition or builds a new one: account selection by account, parent, range, type or role with include and exclude, subtotals, formulas, balance display rules, and full formatting; the standard ones can be reset.

BR-RPT-04 Drill-down to the ledger — Must, Built, source Blueprint, sprint S2. Every figure of a report opens the general ledger for exactly the accounts, period, basis and dimension filter behind it, and the ledger reproduces the figure.

BR-RPT-05 Comparatives, variance and dimension columns — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. Columns combine a period basis (current, year to date, prior period, prior year, as at, custom range), a value basis, an optional dimension filter, and variance columns that compare other columns.

BR-RPT-06 Excel export with formatting — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. Any statement exports to a workbook that carries bold, shading, underline and number formats.

BR-RPT-07 Integrity shown on the page — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S2. A balance sheet states whether assets equal liabilities plus equity, a trial balance whether debits equal credits, and accounts with a balance that no row covers are listed with a warning, so money cannot go missing silently.

BR-RPT-08 Ledger versus documents — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. The ledger is compared with the document-based reports account by account, and a consistency check proves the accounting rules (books balance, balances tie to registers, locked periods never move, subledgers tie to control accounts).

BR-RPT-09 Server-side, company-scoped figures — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S1. Every report figure is computed on the server for the one company of the request, with no row cap and never from a client-supplied company.

BR-RPT-10 Branch-restricted reading — Should, Built, source Standard, sprint S2. A user restricted to branches sees ledger figures of those branches only and is told so.

BR-RPT-11 Printed financial statements — Should, Planned, source Should, sprint S19. Statements print on the company header with page setup, comparatives and signature block, in English, Arabic or Urdu.

BR-RPT-12 Cut-over of document-based statements — Should, Planned, source Decision, sprint S19. After the ledger and the documents agree on every company for an agreed period, the document-based statements are retired one at a time in favour of the ledger statements.

AR — Accounts receivable

BR-AR-01 Invoices and credit notes post to receivables — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AR-01, source Blueprint, sprint S6. A posted invoice, credit note or debit note generates a balanced entry against the customer control account, tagged with the customer.

BR-AR-02 Customer posting profile — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S3. A customer can name its own control account and default dimensions; documents for that customer post through them automatically, and a customer without a profile uses the company default.

BR-AR-03 Receipt across invoices or on account — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AR-02, source Blueprint, sprint S7. One customer receipt settles several invoices at once, or is held on account, through a payment allocation record; it is one voucher and one ledger entry.

BR-AR-04 Apply money held on account — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AR-02, source Blueprint, sprint S7. Money held on account for a customer can be applied later to open invoices, in one or more steps, never more than is held or owed.

BR-AR-05 Cancel a receipt — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S7. A receipt is cancelled with a reason: its entry is reversed, a reversal voucher is written and every settled invoice becomes open again; an invoice settled by a receipt cannot be cancelled until the receipt is.

BR-AR-06 Aging from the ledger — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AR-03, source Blueprint, sprint S6. Receivables aging is a live query over posted lines on the receivable control accounts, oldest charge settled first, with money held on account shown in its own column; it equals the control account to the cent.

BR-AR-07 Customer ledger — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AR-03, source Blueprint, sprint S6. Any customer's postings are listed with opening balance, running balance, document numbers and links to the entries.

BR-AR-08 Document reports follow settlements — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S7. The document-based balance sheet, trial balance, statements, outstanding report and cash flow reflect multi-document receipts and money held on account, including as-at dates in the past.

BR-AR-09 Credit note application — Should, Planned, source Standard, sprint S17. A credit note or customer credit can be applied to other open invoices of the same customer (partial credit memos), with allocation records.

BR-AR-10 Customer statement printing and reminders — Could, Planned, source Standard, sprint S17. A customer statement can be printed or sent and overdue reminders can be produced from the aging.

AP — Accounts payable

BR-AP-01 Goods receipt against clearing — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AP-01, source Blueprint, sprint S6. A goods receipt recognises inventory at cost against a goods-received-not-invoiced account at the moment goods arrive, independent of the vendor bill.

BR-AP-02 Matched bill recognises the payable — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AP-02, source Blueprint, sprint S6. A purchase bill clears the clearing balance, books recoverable VAT and recognises the vendor payable at that moment, not when cash is paid.

BR-AP-03 Expenses share the payable ledger — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AP-03, source Blueprint, sprint S6. An expense without a purchase order posts through the same payable control account and the same vendor subledger as a purchase bill: one vendor position.

BR-AP-04 Vendor posting profile — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S3. A vendor can name its own payable control account and default dimensions, applied automatically to its documents.

BR-AP-05 Payment across bills and expenses — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AP-04, source Blueprint, sprint S7. One vendor payment settles several bills and expenses, or is held on account with the vendor, and can be applied or cancelled exactly like a customer receipt.

BR-AP-06 Payables aging and vendor ledger — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-AP-04, source Blueprint, sprint S6. Payables aging and the vendor ledger mirror the receivables ones, from the ledger.

BR-AP-07 Returns, vendor credit and refunds — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S6. A purchase return posts according to the state of its goods receipt (unbilled, billed, paid); a return after payment creates a vendor credit receivable, cleared by a vendor refund.

BR-AP-08 Approval of large payments — Should, Planned, Blueprint FR-APR-02, source Blueprint, sprint S18. A vendor payment above a threshold waits for approval and releases no funds until every level has approved.

BR-AP-09 Payment runs — Could, Planned, source Standard, sprint S18. Several vendors' due items are selected, approved and paid together in one run.

BNK — Bank reconciliation

BR-BNK-01 Reconciliation session per bank and statement — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-BANK-01, source Blueprint, sprint S8. A reconciliation session exists per bank account per statement date and captures the statement ending balance; only one is in progress per bank and each date is after the last completed one.

BR-BNK-02 Clear ledger lines against statement lines — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-BANK-02, source Blueprint, sprint S8. The session lists every posted, uncleared line of the bank's ledger account; each can be ticked off or matched to a statement line that was typed or imported, with suggested matches that are never applied silently.

BR-BNK-03 Live reconciling formula and blocked completion — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-BANK-03, source Blueprint, sprint S8. The session shows statement balance + deposits in transit - payments outstanding against the book balance live, and cannot be completed while the difference is not zero or a statement line is unmatched.

BR-BNK-04 Discrepancies by journal entry — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-BANK-04, source Blueprint, sprint S8. A bank fee or bank interest found on the statement is posted from inside the session as an entry to an expense or income account, cleared and matched at once; a session is never force-completed.

BR-BNK-05 Completed reconciliation locks its lines — Must, Built, Blueprint FR-BANK-05, source Blueprint, sprint S8. A completed session locks its cleared lines (the document behind one cannot be cancelled); reopening needs its own permission and a reason, applies only to the latest completed session of a bank, and is audited.

BR-BNK-06 Statement import — Should, Built, source Decision, sprint S8. Statement lines can be imported from a CSV file (day-first or year-first dates, signed or debit/credit columns) after a preview, and re-importing the same file adds nothing.

BR-BNK-07 Excel statement import and saved formats — Could, Planned, source Standard, sprint S20. Excel statements import directly and a bank's column layout is remembered.

BR-BNK-08 Bank charges and interest in every statement — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S8. Bank fees and interest found while reconciling appear in the profit and loss, trial balance, month-close figure, dashboard profit and cash flow, so the document-based statements still balance.

TAX — Tax and rounding postings

BR-TAX-01 VAT posted to its own accounts — Must, Partial, Blueprint FR-TAX-01, source Blueprint, sprint S14. Output and recoverable input VAT post to dedicated accounts, never blended into revenue or expense; each line carries its tax code.

BR-TAX-02 VAT settlement entry — Should, Planned, Blueprint FR-TAX-02, source Blueprint, sprint S14c. Filing a quarter posts a settlement entry netting output against input VAT into VAT payable.

BR-TAX-03 Line rounding and cash rounding — Should, Partial, Blueprint FR-RND-01, FR-RND-02, source Blueprint, sprint S14b. Line tax is computed at higher precision and rounded per line; cash-tender rounding never changes the legal total and posts to a rounding account.

BR-TAX-04 Tax codes with components and effective-dated rates — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-TAX-01, source Standard, sprint S14. A tax code is the treatment a line receives. It bundles one or more components (VAT, import VAT, reverse-charge VAT, withholding, excise, customs duty), each with a rate that has a valid-from and valid-to date. A document keeps the code and the rate it was taxed with; a later rate change never alters it.

BR-TAX-05 Tax accounts per code and tagged tax lines — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-TAX-01, source Blueprint, sprint S14. Each component of a tax code names the accounts it posts to: output, recoverable input, non-recoverable treatment, reverse-charge pair, clearing and the authority payable. Every tax line in the ledger carries the tax code, the component, the direction, the recoverable amount and the return box.

BR-TAX-06 Existing tax slabs become tax codes — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint S14. Every tax slab of every company becomes one tax code of the matching supply nature, with no change to any existing document, ledger entry, e-invoice or filed return. After the migration the tax code is the only source of tax treatment; the old slab column is read-only.

BR-TAX-07 Party, item and company tax profiles — Must, Planned, source Standard, sprint S14b. A customer and a vendor carry a tax profile (registration, residency class domestic, GCC or foreign, designated zone, withholding applicability, exemption or treaty certificate with expiry). An item carries a tax class (standard, zero-rated, exempt, excise category, recoverability class, and for goods the customs tariff code and duty rate). A company carries its tax registrations, rounding method, price basis and recovery method.

BR-TAX-08 Automatic tax determination — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint S14b. The system determines the tax code of every line from the transaction type, the party profile, the item class, the place and the date, through ordered rules where the most specific rule wins. A line with no matching rule is refused with a message naming the missing rule; the system never silently posts zero tax. The operator chooses neither the tax treatment nor any account.

BR-TAX-09 Authorised override of a determined tax code — Must, Planned, source Standard, sprint S14b. A user holding the override right may change the determined tax code of a line only with a reason. The override is audited and visible on the document and in the registers; a user without the right cannot change it, through the screen or the API.

BR-TAX-10 Tax-inclusive and tax-exclusive pricing — Should, Planned, Blueprint FR-RND-01, source Standard, sprint S14b. A company, price list or item may price tax-inclusive. The system extracts the tax from the price per line, rounds per line, and the document total equals the price the customer sees.

BR-TAX-11 Zero-rated, exempt and out-of-scope are different supplies — Must, Planned, source Compliance, sprint S14b. The supply nature (standard, zero-rated, exempt, out of scope) is a property of the tax code and is carried on the ledger tags, the return and the e-invoice category with its exemption reason code. A zero-rated supply keeps the right to recover input tax; an exempt supply does not.

BR-TAX-12 Recoverable and non-recoverable input tax — Should, Planned, source Standard, sprint S14c. Input tax is split at posting into the recoverable part (an asset until set off) and the non-recoverable part (added to the cost of the purchase), by the item class, category blocks and the company recovery method, including a percentage apportioned between taxable and exempt supplies and computed from the ledger.

BR-TAX-13 VAT return and registers from ledger tax tags — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-TAX-01, source Blueprint, sprint S14c. The VAT return boxes, the sales, purchase, import, reverse-charge and withholding registers are computed on the server from the ledger tax tags and reconcile to the documents; the consistency check compares the two.

BR-TAX-14 Reverse charge on imported services — Must, Planned, source Compliance, sprint S14d. A service bought from a non-resident vendor is determined as an import of services: output tax is self-assessed and, where the company is entitled, deducted as input tax in the same entry and in the prescribed return boxes.

BR-TAX-15 Withholding tax on payments to non-residents — Should, Planned, source Compliance, sprint S14d. A payment to a non-resident for a service class that attracts withholding deducts tax at the rate of that class, posts the liability to the authority with its own due date, and pays the vendor the net. A treaty certificate on the vendor profile lowers the rate only while it is valid. The payee receives a withholding record.

BR-TAX-16 Excise and other tax components — Could, Planned, source Compliance, sprint S14f. A line may carry an excise component (and other configured components) posting to its own accounts, included in the VAT base where the law says so.

BR-TAX-17 Governance of tax setup — Must, Planned, source Standard, sprint S14. Tax codes, rules, profiles and overrides have their own permissions (create, read, update, deactivate), are audited, are company-scoped with row-level security, and a tax code used by any document can only be deactivated, never deleted. Changes after a VAT quarter is filed apply only to later dates.

PRT — Printed accounting documents

BR-PRT-01 Receipt and payment voucher — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S9. A receipt or payment prints as a voucher with party, amount in words, bank, settled documents, amount held on account, accounting entry and signatures; a cancelled one is stamped and shows its reversing entry.

BR-PRT-02 Journal voucher — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S9. Any manual journal (in any status) and any posted entry prints as a journal voucher with accounts, parties, dimensions, totals in words and the approval trail.

BR-PRT-03 Bank reconciliation statement — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S9. A reconciliation prints as the standard statement: balance per bank, deposits in transit, payments outstanding, adjusted balance, balance per books and the outstanding lists.

BR-PRT-04 Right-to-left printing — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S9. Arabic and Urdu users print these documents right to left, with Arabic account names where they exist.

SEC — Security, permissions and audit

BR-SEC-01 Tenant isolation — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S1. A company's data is invisible to every other company at the database level (row-level security on every table, composite company keys) as well as in the application.

BR-SEC-02 Permission model — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S1. Every module offers create, read, update and delete (delete meaning cancel or deactivate) plus action-specific rights where the authority differs (approve, post, reverse, back-date, override lock, adjust, complete, reopen); a user's rights are the union of their roles and are enforced on the server.

BR-SEC-03 Audit trail of elevated actions — Must, Built, source Standard, sprint S1. Posting overrides, back-dated postings, reopenings, cancellations, approvals, mapping and policy changes are recorded with user, company, time and details.

BR-SEC-04 No bulk overwrite — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S1. There is no endpoint that overwrites documents in bulk; every document is written only by its own route, which applies the period rules.

LOC — Localisation

BR-LOC-01 English, Arabic and Urdu — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. Every screen, message and printed accounting document is available in English, Arabic and Urdu with professional accounting vocabulary.

BR-LOC-02 Right-to-left layout — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. Arabic and Urdu mirror the layout correctly (tables, totals, drawers, prints).

BR-LOC-03 Amount in words in Arabic and Urdu — Could, Planned, source Standard, sprint S20. Amounts in words print in the user's language.

NFR — Platform and quality

BR-NFR-01 Scale — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S1. The design serves thousands of concurrent users across many companies: row-locks scoped to one company, indexed company filters, no cross-tenant serialisation.

BR-NFR-02 Atomic requests — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S1. Every request is one database transaction: a failure anywhere leaves nothing half-written.

BR-NFR-03 Real-database automated tests — Must, Built, source Decision, sprint S9. Behaviour is proven by automated tests over real HTTP and real PostgreSQL, with no mocks; every new feature ships with tests.

BR-NFR-04 Safe releases and environments — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint S0. Live (1.0) and Demo (2.0) are separate tracks; releases run by script with a read-the-plan database step, row-level-security and constraint verification, and a rollback path.

BR-NFR-05 Maintainable documentation — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint S10, S10, S10, S10, S10, S10, S10. The requirements, design, test and sprint documents of the ledger core are generated from one traceable source and kept in the repository.

BR-NFR-06 Promotion to Live — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint S20. The Demo release is promoted to the Live system in a controlled release, with data migration, back-fill and verification on a copy of production first.

IMP — Imports, landed cost and foreign currency

BR-IMP-01 Foreign-currency documents and exchange differences — Must, Planned, source Blueprint, sprint S14e. A purchase document may be in a foreign currency with the rate of its date stored on it. Tax and inventory post in the company currency; a payment at another rate posts the exchange difference; a missing rate is refused.

BR-IMP-02 Landed cost — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-LC-01, FR-LC-02, FR-LC-04, source Blueprint, sprint S14e. Any number of cost lines (freight, duty, insurance, clearing agent, bank charges) can be added to a goods receipt, each capitalised into stock or expensed by default and overridable, allocated to items by value, quantity or manually, so that the average cost used for cost of sales is the fully landed cost.

BR-IMP-03 Customs entry — Must, Planned, source Compliance, sprint S14e. An import is recorded by a customs entry: customs value, duty and import VAT payable to the customs authority, with the recoverable import VAT posted to input tax and any non-recoverable part added to the stock cost.

BR-IMP-04 Import of goods is determined automatically — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint S14e. A purchase of goods from a vendor whose profile is foreign is treated as an import of goods without the operator choosing it: the expected duty and import VAT are shown and the customs entry is requested at receipt.

BR-IMP-05 Several payees per shipment — Must, Planned, Blueprint FR-LC-03, source Blueprint, sprint S14e. The goods vendor, the freight forwarder, the customs authority, the insurer and the clearing agent each become their own payable, visible in payables aging, none forced onto the goods bill.

FND — Core foundations (events, ledgers, currency, references, quantities, schedules, determination)

BR-FND-01 Accounting events layer — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF1. A document raises an accounting event; accounting rules turn the event into balanced ledger lines with a preview and can re-derive them; every existing posting path moves onto this layer without changing its result.

BR-FND-02 Parallel ledgers — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF2. A company can keep more than one ledger (for example IFRS, local statutory, tax) fed by the same events, each with its own rules and period status.

BR-FND-03 Currency — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF2. Transaction, functional and reporting currencies; dated exchange rates; documents in foreign currency store the rate and the amounts in each currency; settlement posts exchange differences; open items and balances are revalued by a run.

BR-FND-04 Typed business-object references — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF3. A ledger line can reference any registered business object (party, item, asset, project, order, property, unit, lease, fund) through one typed, validated mechanism instead of one column per subledger.

BR-FND-05 Quantities on ledger lines — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF3. A ledger line can carry a quantity and unit of measure (hours, units, area) for rates, per-unit cost, capacity and statistical drivers; reports can show quantity and rate as value bases.

BR-FND-06 Recognition schedules — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF8. One schedule engine releases an amount over time or by progress (deferred income, prepaid cost, depreciation, lease income, percentage of completion) through previewed, approved and period-locked runs.

BR-FND-07 Account determination rules — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF5. Rules determine accounts from the transaction type, party class, item class, branch and date (and any registered attribute) so that documents name only who and what; one rule engine serves accounts and tax codes.

BR-FND-08 Segregation of duties and master-data approval — Should, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF9. Conflicting duties are defined and enforced (for example create a vendor and change its bank account), and changes to sensitive master data (bank details, credit limit, tax profile) can require approval.

BUD — Budgeting and commitment control

BR-BUD-01 Budget versions — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF7. Budgets are held in versions (original, revised, forecast) by account or account group, dimension combination and fiscal period, with optional quantity.

BR-BUD-02 Budget control — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF7. A company chooses which dimension types a control applies to and the mode (report, warn or stop; warn by default) at posting, requisition and order.

BR-BUD-03 Commitments — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF7. Open requisitions and orders count against the budget with their dimension combination until invoiced.

BR-BUD-04 Budget versus actual reporting — Must, Planned, source Decision, sprint SF7. Budget is a value basis of the report designer so that budget, actual, prior year and variance can be shown for any dimension.

SAL — Sales documents (quotations, invoices, credit notes)

BR-SAL-01 Quotations convert to invoices — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A quotation can be created, edited, cancelled and converted into a tax invoice. A converted quotation is final. The conversion is an ordinary invoice and follows every invoice rule (numbering, period locks, e-invoicing).

BR-SAL-02 Invoice payments, in part or in full — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An invoice can be paid in several payments. Each payment is its own receipt voucher. A payment is limited to the remaining balance; a zero or negative amount, a cancelled invoice, an already paid invoice and a credit note cannot be paid. Only a user with the create right can record a payment, and only in the invoice's own company.

BR-SAL-03 Cancelling an invoice — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An invoice can be cancelled in an open period; the receipt voucher of a paid invoice is reversed by a reversal voucher, never deleted. An invoice dated in a filed VAT quarter cannot be cancelled; a ZATCA-cleared invoice cannot be cancelled (see BR-ZAT-02).

BR-SAL-04 Credit notes — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A credit note reverses an invoice. Only one credit note can be raised against an invoice. When the original was paid, the credit note reverses its receipt; when unpaid, no voucher is touched. Credit notes follow the approval rules when one matches.

BR-SAL-05 Sales associate on an invoice — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An invoice can name the employee who sold it. The employee must belong to the same company and be active; the field is optional.

EXP — Expenses and recurring postings

BR-EXP-01 Expense payments, in part or in full — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An expense can be paid in several payments, each its own payment voucher, never above the remaining balance. Paid or cancelled expenses cannot be paid, and another company's expense is never found.

BR-EXP-02 Cancelling an expense — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An expense can be cancelled once, by a user with the cancel right. A paid expense is cancelled with a reversal voucher. An expense dated in a filed VAT quarter cannot be cancelled.

BR-EXP-03 Recurring templates and accruals — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A company keeps recurring expense templates (create, edit, switch off) and accrual entries (edit, cancel). Amounts must be valid; nothing is hard-deleted: a template is deactivated and an accrual cancelled, together with its linked recurring posting.

PUR — Procurement and inventory

BR-PUR-01 Purchase bills from goods receipts — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A purchase bill is raised from goods receipt notes of one vendor. Its totals are computed on the server from the receipts, a receipt can be billed only once, a reversed receipt cannot be billed, and only a user with the create right can bill. A bill that matches an approval rule waits for approval.

BR-PUR-02 Purchase bill payment and cancellation — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A bill can be paid in part and then in full, never above the balance. An unpaid bill can be cancelled, which releases its receipts for re-billing; a bill with a payment cannot be cancelled. A bill dated in a filed VAT quarter cannot be cancelled.

BR-PUR-03 Purchase returns — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Goods can be returned to a vendor against a receipt: never more than was received or than remains after earlier returns, never against a reversed receipt. A return reduces stock and can be cancelled once (restoring stock). A return cannot be created while the current VAT quarter is filed.

BR-PUR-04 Physical stock takes — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A stock take snapshots the system quantity on the server and computes the variance. Finalising posts the counted quantity as the stock level, once. A draft can be cancelled; a completed one cannot.

BR-PUR-05 Warehouse transfers — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Stock moves between warehouses by a dispatch and a receiving. Dispatch deducts the source, receiving adds the quantity actually received, once even under concurrent requests. A dispatch still in transit can be cancelled to restore stock. Branch-restricted users act only on their own warehouses, and cross-company warehouses are refused.

POS — Point of sale

BR-POS-01 Cashier shifts — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A cashier opens and closes a shift, recording actual and expected cash. Shifts are listed and closed only within their own company; opening and closing need the shift right and using the terminal needs the access right.

BR-POS-02 Held sales — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A sale in progress can be held and later resumed. Held sales are visible only within their company and need the terminal access right.

BR-POS-03 POS sales are invoices — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. A POS sale is an ordinary tax invoice and goes through the same e-invoicing pipeline; a POS cancel needs its own permission.

ZAT — ZATCA e-invoicing

BR-ZAT-01 E-invoicing is a per-company switch — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. E-invoicing is off for a company until its onboarding enables it. With it off, an invoice lands as DISABLED with a real basic QR code and never touches the ZATCA gateway or the hash chain; with it on, the same invoice proceeds.

BR-ZAT-02 Cancellation of e-invoiced documents — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. An invoice that reached ZATCA (cleared or reported) or is being submitted cannot be cancelled; a credit note is the only reversal. An invoice that never reached ZATCA can be cancelled.

BR-ZAT-03 Hash-chain integrity — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. Every document of a company and environment sits on one hash chain that starts at genesis for each new company and environment. Resubmitting the tip reuses its position; resubmitting an earlier document after a later one mints a fresh position; cancelling the tip that never reached ZATCA gives its position back; a cleared document cannot be resubmitted; the issue time is locked once set.

BR-ZAT-04 Safe storage of keys and signed documents — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. ZATCA private keys are encrypted at rest (authenticated encryption, random IV, tamper detection, clear error when the secret is missing or wrong). The signed XML is stored compressed, losslessly, and is served on demand, never in list payloads, and never to another tenant.

VAT — VAT returns and period locks

BR-VAT-01 VAT return figures — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. A quarterly VAT return is generated from the documents: sales, output VAT, purchases and input VAT, with credit notes netted. The return and the VAT summary report show the same numbers. A generated return can be deleted and regenerated, and a duplicate for the same quarter is refused.

BR-VAT-02 Filing rules — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. A return cannot be generated or filed while an invoice in its quarter has not completed e-invoicing. Quarters must be filed in order; a filed return is permanently locked.

BR-VAT-03 A filed quarter locks its documents — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. Once a quarter is filed (and while a generated return freezes it), no invoice, credit note, expense or purchase bill can be created in it, and none can be cancelled or converted into it. Quotations, requisitions, orders and stock takes are not locked.

BR-VAT-04 VAT return rights and isolation — Must, Built, source Compliance, sprint SB1. Reading, generating, filing and deleting a return are separate rights, and a company can never see or touch another company's returns.

MST — Master data, numbering and imports

BR-MST-01 Document numbering — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Every document type has its own numbering in the PREFIX-1001 style, issued sequentially with no duplicates or gaps even under concurrent requests, per document period, continuing from the legacy counter. Changing the policy needs the right and cannot change e-invoicing settings.

BR-MST-02 Customer and vendor import — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Customers and vendors can be loaded from a spreadsheet: a template, validation before saving (a business customer needs a valid VAT number and full address), update of an existing record matched by VAT number or phone, conflict detection, a row cap and the create right.

BR-MST-03 Product import — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Products can be loaded from a spreadsheet: template, validation without writing, categories resolved by name in the company, real SKUs assigned, an existing barcode updates instead of duplicating, a row cap and the create right.

BR-MST-04 Master data rights and retirement — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Master data uses create, read, update and delete rights, where delete means making a record inactive. Create does not allow editing an existing row and update does not allow creating one; an inactive record cannot be edited. Cancel sets a flag without changing the status and works once.

ORG — Tenancy, users and company administration

BR-ORG-01 Every request belongs to one company — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Data returned and written is always for the company of the request: a user sees their own company; only a super-administrator can switch company, and the choice persists in the session. A company can never overwrite another company's customer, invoice, quotation or expense, and reports and dashboards are computed per company.

BR-ORG-02 User accounts — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Administrators can deactivate and reactivate users of their own company (never their own session), assign roles of the same company only, and a delegated user creating accounts can only create ordinary staff. Passwords need at least six characters with a letter and a digit.

BR-ORG-03 Employees and job titles — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Employees have a job title, an employee number issued in sequence (width and start configurable) and an optional branch. Duplicate active titles and numbers are refused, a deactivated title cannot be used, and a new login account must link to an active employee once the company has employees.

BR-ORG-04 Sessions and sign-in — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. A session records the user and company at sign-in, expires, and is extended once past half of its lifetime. Only a super-administrator can list sessions across companies.

BR-ORG-05 No bulk save of documents — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. There is no endpoint that overwrites posted documents in bulk. A document is written only through its own route, and a request naming an existing id cannot take over another document's lines.

BR-ORG-06 Capital contributions — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. An investor's capital contribution can be recorded: it creates a receipt voucher and adds to capital contributed, accumulating across contributions. Amounts must be positive, banks active and investors in the company.

REP — Reports and dashboard (documents)

BR-REP-01 Server-side, paged and exact reports — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Every register, statement and report is computed on the server for the active company. Long lists are paged in the database with the full count and totals over every row, with a no-paging path for print and export. An end date includes its whole last day, an invalid date is refused, a branch-restricted user never sees other branches, and a user without the report right is refused.

BR-REP-02 Reports agree with the documents and with each other — Must, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Profit and loss, cash flow, balance sheet, trial balance, VAT summary, KPI cards, outstanding balances, the dashboard, the stock register and the ledger itself all reconcile to the source documents on a real book of invoices, credit notes, expenses and bills. Refunded money is not counted as collected and credited invoices are not pending.

CFG — Document templates and translations

BR-CFG-01 Print templates and translations — Should, Built, source Platform, sprint SB1. Each company has its own print templates (create, edit options and layout, activate one per language, delete); only an administrator manages them and no template can be changed across companies. Translations are one shared table managed only by a super-administrator, per key.

7. Constraints and assumptions

  • Single company currency (SAR); multi-currency and intercompany are out of scope.
  • Live (1.0) is changed by fixes only; all development is on the 2.0 track and reaches Live only by an explicit promotion (BR-NFR-06).
  • Existing document-based reports remain the figures of record until the cut-over (BR-RPT-12).
  • ZATCA e-invoicing code is not altered by this programme.
  • Document dates, posting guard and permission model are platform standards that every later feature must follow.

8. Success criteria

  • Ledger-vs-Documents agrees to the cent for every company; the consistency check shows no error-level failure.
  • Every built requirement has automated test coverage traced in the matrix (08).
  • A new company can complete the user-manual flow (06) without developer help.

Chapter 7

Software Requirements Specification

Source: docs/gl-ap-ar/02-software-requirements-specification.md

Note

Generated by scripts/build-gl-docs.mjs from docs/gl-ap-ar/source. Do not edit; change the source and rebuild.

Version 1.0 / 2026-10-15. Satisfies the BRD (01). Each system requirement (FR) states what the system shall do, where it is implemented, and which test cases prove it (case ids resolve in docs/test-cases/).

1. System overview

A multi-tenant web application (React client, Node/Express API, PostgreSQL with row-level security). Documents (invoices, expenses, bills, vouchers, settlements) and manual journals post through one engine, postJournalEntry, into journal_entries / journal_lines. Subledgers, aging, statements and every report are queries over those lines. See the solution design (03).

2. Cross-cutting requirements

Id Requirement
SYS-01 Every request shall run in one database transaction scoped to req.targetCompanyId; any error rolls the whole request back.
SYS-02 No route shall accept a client-supplied company id; every client-supplied foreign key shall be checked to belong to the company.
SYS-03 Every dated posting shall pass assertPostingAllowed.
SYS-04 Posted ledger lines shall never be updated or deleted; correction is by reversal.
SYS-05 Every new table shall carry company_id, composite (company_id, id) keys where referenced, RLS, and an index on company_id.
SYS-06 Every new user-visible string shall have English, Arabic and Urdu text.
SYS-07 Money shall be stored as decimal(14,2) and computed with 2-decimal rounding (round2).

3. Functional requirements

DIM — Dimensions and cost centres

Chapter 8

Solution Design Document

Source: docs/gl-ap-ar/03-solution-design-document.md

Version 1.0 / 2026-10-15. Realises the BRD (01) and SRS (02). The detailed mechanics (algorithms, tables, routes) are in the Technical Design (04); this document explains how the solution is shaped and why.

1. Design principles (apply to every future feature)

  1. One place money is recorded. Every module is a source: it decides when and how much, then hands the posting engine a balanced set of lines. AR, AP, bank, trial balance, P&L, balance sheet are queries over the same rows (Blueprint core idea).
  2. A document names who and what, never accounts. The engine resolves control account, bank account, expense-category account and dimension tags from a posting context. No route selects accounts by hand.
  3. Immutable ledger. Posted lines are never edited; a correction is a mirrored reversal plus a new entry, carrying the original tags and parties.
  4. One guard, one engine, one query layer. assertPostingAllowed for every dated posting; postJournalEntry for every write; resolvedLines for every read; reportEngine for every statement.
  5. Tenant isolation twice. Application filter by req.targetCompanyId and PostgreSQL row-level security with composite (company_id, id) keys.
  6. Server-side figures. Nothing a user acts on is computed in the browser or from the capped /api/state.
  7. Architecture first, no patches. A defect is fixed where the rule is enforced (engine, guard, helper), never by adjusting one figure. New behaviour extends the one place that owns the concern.

2. Context

flowchart LR
  subgraph Sources
    INV[Invoices / credit notes / POS]
    EXP[Expenses / recurring]
    PUR[GRN / purchase bills / returns]
    SET[Receipts / vendor payments]
    BNK[Bank reconciliation adjustments]
    MAN[Manual journals / opening balances]
  end
  GUARD[Posting guard<br/>periods, lock date, back-date window]
  ENGINE[Posting engine<br/>postJournalEntry<br/>accounts, parties, dimensions]
  LEDGER[(journal_entries<br/>journal_lines)]
  INV --> GUARD
  EXP --> GUARD
  PUR --> GUARD
  SET --> GUARD
  BNK --> GUARD
  MAN --> GUARD
  GUARD --> ENGINE --> LEDGER
  LEDGER --> SUB[Subledgers<br/>aging, party ledger]
  LEDGER --> REP[Report engine<br/>TB, IS, BS, CF, equity, custom]
  LEDGER --> GL[General ledger / drill-down]
  LEDGER --> CHK[Consistency checks R1-R8]
  DOCS[(Documents and vouchers)] --> LEGACY[Document-based reports<br/>figures of record until cut-over]
  LEDGER -. compared .-> LEGACY

External systems: ZATCA (e-invoicing, outside this programme), SMTP (notifications), the browser print engine.

3. Logical architecture

Layer Responsibility Main elements
Client (React) Screens, forms, print preview src/components/LedgerModule.tsx and ledger/* screens, DocumentRenderer.tsx
API (Express) Authentication, tenant context, permission checks, route handlers server/routes/*, tenantDb.ts (withTenantDb), authz.ts
Domain services Posting, guard, settlements, subledger, reconciliation, reports ledger.ts, postingGuard.ts, postingContext.ts, settlements.ts, settlementFacts.ts, subledger.ts, bankReconciliation.ts, reportEngine.ts, financialReports.ts, postingPrint.ts
Data Tables, RLS, constraints PostgreSQL; src/db/schema.ts single source of truth
Operations Release, migration, back-fill, verification deploy/*, scripts/*

4. Key design decisions (summary; full log in annex H)

# Decision Why
D1 Company-owned chart with 16 posting roles that documents use; mapping is not retroactive Documents stay simple; an accountant can re-point a role without rewriting history
D2 Control accounts by subledger_type; customers/vendors can name their own Several receivable accounts per company (trade, staff) without changing documents
D3 Typed party tags (customer_id, vendor_id) on ledger lines, set by the engine Aging and statements become queries; control account = sum of parties provable to the cent
D4 Dimensions as a combination id per line (deduplicated), account rules, priority of defaults One indexed filter; analytical, never a second ledger
D5 One report-definition engine; five standard statements are definitions No second calculation; designer comes free
D6 doc_date vs posting_date; business dates are date Reports and locks must agree on one meaning of "when"
D7 Posting guard as one function with permission overrides and audit Tier-1 practice: period status + authority + audit, not "days back" alone
D8 A settlement is one voucher + one entry + N allocation rows; remainder on account Matches how banks and customers behave; one cash movement, many documents
D9 settlementFacts is the only translator between allocations and per-document payments for older reports Two shapes of "paid" must never be reconciled in many places
D10 Bank fee/interest is a voucher and an entry, restricted to expense/income accounts Every cash movement is a voucher in this system; older statements must still balance
D11 Reconciliation lock enforced in the reversal function The only door through which a bank line can be undone
D12 Documents print from one server-resolved posting payload Print, screen and ledger cannot disagree
D13 Ledger and document-based reports run side by side until a gated cut-over De-risks go-live; "Ledger vs Documents" proves agreement
D14 Maker-checker approval for manual journals first; amount-threshold rules (Sprint 11) layered on top: a matching rule replaces the single step, no rule leaves it unchanged Control early, no big-bang migration of the journal flow
D15 An approval request holds the intent (payload) of a document, never a half-posted document; the document is created by its own executor when the last level approves Nothing can leak into the ledger, reports or payment runs while waiting; one engine serves every document type
D16 A shortcut that would create and pay a document in one step is refused whole when a rule would hold either step A rule cannot be bypassed by a convenience flow
D17 One company setting decides whether the submitter may approve, for every document type One place, owner/admin only, audited
D18 Proposed: tax determination, computation, posting, reporting and settlement are separate steps; every document calls one tax engine and contains no tax logic A rate on a document cannot also decide accounts, recovery and boxes; one engine gives one behaviour for invoice, POS, bill, expense, receipt and return
D19 Proposed: the tax code is the unit; components with effective-dated rates; accounts hang off the code and component History is never rewritten by a rate change; accounts are looked up, never typed
D20 Proposed: profiles on the party, the item and the company; ordered determination rules, most specific first; no matching rule is an error, never zero; override only with the right and a reason The data entry operator names the party and items; the system decides what to post and where
D21 Proposed: recoverable and non-recoverable input tax are split at posting; apportionment is computed from the ledger Cost and the tax asset are right from the first entry, and a mixed taxable and exempt business is supported
D22 Proposed: the return, the registers and the settlement entry read ledger tax tags and reconcile to documents One set of numbers; the consistency check proves it
D23 Proposed: import of goods is automated only after foreign currency, landed cost and customs entry exist The import entries need those three; building the tax side first would post wrong costs
D24 Proposed: existing slabs migrate to tax codes in one idempotent script; afterwards the slab column is read-only One source of tax treatment, no parallel logic

5. Key flows

5.1 A document posts

sequenceDiagram
  participant U as User
  participant R as Route (withTenantDb)
  participant G as assertPostingAllowed
  participant E as postJournalEntry
  participant DB as PostgreSQL (RLS)
  U->>R: POST invoice / expense / receipt ...
  R->>G: posting date, user, reason
  G-->>R: ok or refusal (period, lock, window, reason)
  R->>E: referenceType, referenceId, lines, context
  E->>E: resolve control account, bank account, party tags, dimension combination
  E->>DB: insert entry + lines (balanced or throw)
  R-->>U: response (one transaction commits or rolls back)

5.2 Receipt across invoices

sequenceDiagram
  participant U as Accountant
  participant S as settlements route
  participant L as Ledger
  U->>S: customer, bank, amount, allocations[]
  S->>S: validate each allocation against open items of that customer
  S->>L: voucher (CustomerReceipt) + entry Dr bank / Cr receivable (customer-tagged)
  S->>S: insert payment_allocations, move invoices' amount_paid / status
  S-->>U: applied + held on account
  Note over S,L: Cancel = reason, reverse entry, Reversal voucher, allocations reversed (reversed_date)

5.3 Bank reconciliation

stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> InProgress: start (bank, date, ending balance)
  InProgress --> InProgress: tick / match lines, import statement, post fee or interest
  InProgress --> Completed: difference = 0 and no unmatched statement line
  Completed --> InProgress: reopen (permission + reason; latest session only; audited)
  InProgress --> [*]: discard (releases cleared lines)

5.4 Manual journal

stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> Draft
  Draft --> Pending: submit (balanced, period ok)
  Pending --> Posted: approve (other person; period re-checked)
  Pending --> Draft: reject (reason)
  Draft --> Discarded
  Posted --> Posted: reverse = new reversing draft, same path

6. Data design (overview)

  • Ledger: journal_entries (reference, dates, reversal link), journal_lines (account or role, bank, dimension combination, customer/vendor tag, reconciliation link).
  • Chart: company_accounts (+ system_accounts roles), dimensions (dimension_*, cost_centers, account_dimension_rules).
  • Documents and cash: invoices, expenses, purchase_bills, vouchers, payment_allocations.
  • Control: posting_policies, fiscal_months, manual_journals, bank_reconciliations, bank_statement_lines, audit_logs.
  • Reporting: report_definitions, report_rows, report_row_selectors, report_columns. Full column-level detail: annex A.

7. Security design

  • Authentication by session; every request gets req.targetCompanyId (super-admin may select a company explicitly).
  • withTenantDb opens one transaction on the restricted erp_app_tenant role and sets app.company_id; RLS policies on every table enforce it, composite keys stop cross-company references.
  • Permissions: module registry (permissionSchema.ts), union of roles, server-enforced, record-state rules separate from permissions (annex C).
  • Audit: elevated actions via recordAuditLog; posting overrides as BACKDATED_POSTING.
  • No bulk-write endpoint exists.

8. Deployment and environments

Live (branch master, production) and Demo (branch v2, port 4000) are separate; releases run by script (deploy/release-demo.sh, deploy/release.sh): backup, pull and build, date migration, unique-key prerequisites, reviewed db:push, RLS predicates, check constraints, system accounts, back-fill, translations, reload and health check. See annex F.

9. Quality design

Real-HTTP + real-PostgreSQL tests (tests/), a 290-case catalogue (docs/test-cases), the consistency check as a runtime invariant, npm run check:isolation as a static tripwire. Strategy: document 05.

10. Risks and mitigations

Risk Mitigation
Two report families disagree Ledger-vs-Documents + consistency rules; gated cut-over (S19)
A new posting path bypasses the guard One guard function; route list in the posting-control doc; test PC-009
Cross-tenant leak RLS + composite keys + isolation tripwire + per-table tests
Schema push drops data Reviewed plan, pre-push keys, date migration idempotent, backup first
Document drift This set is generated from one traceable source and validated (build-gl-docs.mjs --check)

Taxation architecture (proposed, Sprints 14 to 14f; nothing built)

The full analysis, the tier-1 comparison, the scenarios (retail, imports, exports, manufacturing) and the open decisions are in the design note Taxation and tax profiles (chapter in the technical documentation). In one paragraph: a document names the party and the items; the tax engine looks up the party tax profile, the item tax class, the company tax profile and the transaction type, matches an ordered determination rule and returns a tax code; the code gives the components, the rates valid on the document date, the recoverability and the accounts; the posting engine writes the tax lines with their tags; the return, the registers and the settlement entry read those tags. The operator never chooses a tax or an account.

Principle (decision) Requirement ids Sprint
One engine; codes, components, dated rates, accounts per code (D18, D19) BR-TAX-04, BR-TAX-05, BR-TAX-17 14
Slabs migrate to codes (D24) BR-TAX-06 14
Profiles, determination, override, supply natures, tax-inclusive pricing (D20) BR-TAX-07..11, BR-TAX-03 14b
Recovery, return from the ledger, settlement entry (D21, D22) BR-TAX-12, BR-TAX-13, BR-TAX-02 14c
Reverse charge and withholding BR-TAX-14, BR-TAX-15 14d
Foreign currency, landed cost, customs entry, automatic import (D23) BR-IMP-01..05 14e
Excise and other components BR-TAX-16 14f

Chapter 9

Technical Design Document

Source: docs/gl-ap-ar/04-technical-design-document.md

Version 1.0 / 2026-10-15. Audience: developers. Companion to the Solution Design (03); data model in annex A, routes in annex B, permissions in annex C. Paths are relative to the repository root.

1. Technology

React 19 + Vite client, Node/Express API (tsx server.ts in development, esbuild bundle in production), PostgreSQL + drizzle-orm, vitest (real HTTP + real Postgres), ExcelJS (exports), Playwright (PDF), PM2 + Nginx on the VPS. Schema is pushed with npm run db:push (no migration files) — see section 14.

2. Module map

Concern File(s)
Posting engine server/lib/ledger.ts (postJournalEntry, reverseAllEntriesFor, assertNoReconciledLines)
Posting context and profiles server/lib/postingContext.ts, server/lib/partyProfile.ts
Posting guard server/lib/postingGuard.ts, route ledgerPostingPolicy.ts
Document dates server/lib/documentDates.ts, server/lib/pickFields.ts
Chart, mapping server/lib/chartOfAccounts.ts, server/routes/ledgerAccounts.ts
Manual journals, opening server/routes/ledgerJournals.ts, ledgerOpening.ts

Ledger reads server/lib/ledgerQueries.ts (resolvedLines, accountTotals, branchIdsFor)

Subledgers server/lib/subledger.ts, server/routes/ledgerSubledger.ts
