Translations src/glTranslations.ts, scripts/apply-gl-translations.ts

3. Request lifecycle

isAuthenticated (session or x-session-id) → sets req.user, req.targetCompanyId → route withTenantDb (one transaction on role erp_app_tenant, set_config('app.company_id', …, true); commit/rollback on res.end) → permission check (hasPermission(req.user, 'module.leaf')) → ownership checks of every client-supplied id → assertPostingAllowed (dated postings) → domain service via tenantDb() → recordAuditLog → response. Never wrap a handler body in another db.transaction. runAfterTenantCommit defers side effects (ZATCA) until commit.

4. Posting engine

postJournalEntry(tx, { companyId, branchId, postingDate, docDate?, referenceType, referenceId, description, createdById, lines[], dimensions?, dimensionMode?, context? }).

  1. Sum debits/credits; refuse imbalance beyond the rounding ceiling (a sub-cent difference goes to the ROUNDING role).
  2. loadPostingProfile from the posting context (document party read from the source row by partyOfDocument, or passed explicitly): control account per customer/vendor, bank account per bankId, expense-category account, default dimension combinations.
  3. Each line resolves to an account: explicit accountId → bank account (for role BANK with bankId) → party control account → role mapping (company_accounts.system_key).
  4. Dimension tags merge by priority: line → entry → party → bank → account default; applyRules then drops not-allowed tags and (mode strict, manual journals) refuses missing required ones; resolveCombination find-or-creates one deduplicated combination.
  5. resolvePartyTags sets customer_id / vendor_id on every line whose account has subledger_type customer/vendor.
  6. Insert entry and lines; return the entry id.

reverseAllEntriesFor(tx, companyId, referenceType, referenceId, date, description, userId) finds every unreversed entry of the document, first calls assertNoReconciledLines (a bank line cleared by a reconciliation blocks the whole reversal), then posts each mirror with the original accounts, bank, tags and parties. journal_entries.reversal_of_id is unique, so an entry reverses once.

5. Posting guard (assertPostingAllowed(req, postingDate, { vat, reason }))

Order: (1) open existing fiscal month, not future; (2) VAT quarter (vat: 'none' | 'filed' | 'frozen'); (3) posting_policies.lock_date unless postingControl.overrideLock; (4) max_back_days unless postingControl.backDate; (5) reason when older than reason_after_days; (6) audit BACKDATED_POSTING when an override or a reason was used. No policy row = rules 1–2 only. Every route listed in docs/design-notes/posting-control.md calls it; manual journals call it at submit and approval; settlements and bank adjustments call it from the route.

6. Document dates

doc_date (document), posting_date (ledger; defaults to doc_date via documentDates()), <meaning>_date as Postgres date via the businessDate custom type (string YYYY-MM-DD; raw tx.execute also returns strings). Renames were done in place by scripts/migrate-date-fields.mjs before db:push. Naming rules: docs/design-notes/date-fields-standard.md and skill consistent-column-names.

7. Settlements (server/lib/settlements.ts)

  • openItems(kind, partyId) reuses computeOutstanding (one definition of "owed").
  • recordSettlement: validate amount, bank, party, allocations (checkAllocations: type allowed for the kind, amount > 0, document open for that party, ≤ outstanding, no duplicates, total ≤ amount) → voucher (CustomerReceipt Receipt / VendorPayment Payment, reference_id = party id) → entry (bank Dr / AR Cr, or AP Dr / bank Cr; the engine tags the party) → payment_allocations rows (applied_date) → movePaid updates the document (amount_paid, status; bills use status).
  • applyOnAccount locks the voucher row and allocates up to amount − Σ active allocations.
  • cancelSettlement requires a reason: moves paid amounts back, sets reversed_at/reversed_date, reverseAllEntriesFor, writes a Reversal voucher with reversal_of_voucher_id.
  • assertNoActiveAllocations is called by invoice cancel and cancelExpense.

8. Allocations in the document-based reports (settlementFacts.ts)

allocationEvents turns each allocation into the voucher it would have been had it been paid directly (type Receipt/Payment dated applied_date; a Reversal dated reversed_date). Used for: later-payment adjustment of as-at balance sheets (laterVoucherTotals), cash-basis revenue/expense recognition, outstanding figures. onAccountBalances(asOf) = settlement vouchers up to the date not cancelled by then, minus allocations applied and not reversed by then → Customer advances (liability) / Vendor advances (asset) in computeBalanceSheet and computeTrialBalance; Ledger-vs-Documents compares AR/AP net of them. Statements and cash flow read the settlement voucher itself (full amount, own date). Reversal direction is one rule: reversalIsCashOut(referenceType).

9. Subledger (server/lib/subledger.ts)

Balance per party = Σ(debit − credit) of lines tagged with the party on the control accounts. Aging = balance-forward: charges aged from their document date, credits (payments, credit notes, receipts on account) set against the oldest charge first; an unapplied credit shows in an On account column; buckets Not due / 1–30 / 31–60 / 61–90 / Over 90 (labels from the report). reconciliation returns control-account balance, aging total, difference and untagged lines. Consistency rules R8.1–R8.4 prove: no untagged control line, parties sum to control accounts, aging equals balance.

10. Bank reconciliation (server/lib/bankReconciliation.ts)

  • Bank ledger account = company_accounts.bank_id; its lines are resolvedLines with that account id.
  • Lines shown for a session: outstanding at its date (bank_reconciliation_id IS NULL or cleared by a later session) plus those it cleared.
  • Summary: book = Σ lines ≤ date; cleared = Σ lines cleared by this session or an earlier Completed one; difference = statement − cleared; deposits in transit / payments outstanding = uncleared debits / credits. Identity: statement + deposits in transit − payments outstanding = book when difference = 0.
  • Completion: difference = 0 and no unmatched statement line; stores gl_balance_at_reconciliation.
  • Adjustment: voucher (BankCharge Payment or BankInterest Receipt) + entry (bank against an active, postable Expense (charge) or Revenue (interest) account), line cleared and matched. Reversal: reverseAdjustment un-matches, un-clears, reverses entry, writes Reversal voucher.
  • Legacy statements read the charge/interest figures from the ledger entry: bankAdjustmentRows/Totals in financialReports.ts feed P&L, trial balance, balance sheet (open months), month P&L, dashboard and cash flow.
  • Reopen: latest completed session of the bank only; no other session in progress; audit BANK_RECONCILIATION_REOPEN.

11. Report engine (server/lib/reportEngine.ts)

Definition = rows × columns. Rows: Heading, Accounts (selectors: account, parent rollup, range, type, role; include/exclude; value rule Net/Debit/Credit/Debit-only/Credit-only/Split; show rule), Subtotal, Calculated (formula over row numbers), Text, Spacer, ProfitToDate. Columns: periodBasis × valueBasis × presentation × optional dimension filter, plus variance columns. Per column the engine fetches per-account debit/credit totals once through resolvedLines. Every cell returns { accountIds, from, to, dims, basis } for drill-down. Formatting is validated by one shared module. The legacy /reports/trial-balance is an adapter over the seeded definition.

11a. Approvals (server/lib/approvals.ts, server/routes/approvals.ts)

  • Tables: approval_rules (document type, amount band: above min_amount up to and including max_amount, priority), approval_rule_levels (1..5; approver type Role / User / Permission / Manager with manager_steps), approval_requests (intent payload, rule snapshot levels, current level, status, submitter, self_approved, result_document_id), approval_actions (append-only). users.manager_id feeds the Manager approver; the users route (server/routes/users.ts) validates it (same company, not self, no loop up to 20 hops) and the user form in Settings sets it.
  • submitForApproval(tx, req, {documentType, amount, summary, payload}): lowest-priority active rule whose band contains the amount; none = {pending:false} and the caller continues exactly as before.
  • decideRequest: row-locks the request; refuses the submitter unless companies.journal_self_approval is on; checks the level authority (holdsLevelAuthority), that the actor has not acted before, requires remarks to reject; on the last approval calls the registered executor, which runs assertPostingAllowed for the approver and creates the document as the submitter. Any refusal rolls back the whole decision (no action row is kept).
  • Recall: recallRequest (submitter only, pending only; action row Recalled, status Recalled). Delegation: approval_delegations (delegator = signed-in user, valid_from..valid_to, active flag, delegator <> delegate); levelAuthority returns the person's own authority or the delegator they act for (role, user and manager levels only), the decision records on_behalf_of_id.
  • Credit notes: the credit/debit note route was split into loadNoteOriginal (existence, branch, not cancelled, not credited twice, has lines; throws with a status) and issueInvoiceNote (document, lines, restock, money reversal, ledger reversal, ZATCA after commit), used by the route and by the CreditNote executor, so the approved note is exactly the one the route would have issued; the amount judged is the original invoice total.
  • Manual journals: submit calls submitForApproval('Journal', total debit); a match creates a request (payload journalId) while the journal stays Pending; the approve/reject routes look up the open request (openJournalRequest) and decide through the engine, otherwise run the unchanged single-step logic; the posting code is postManualJournal, shared by the approve route and the Journal executor.
  • Executors: VendorPayment (settlements route, recordSettlement), VendorBill (inventory route, createPurchaseBillForGrns). A bill's amount comes from purchaseBillTotals (the same function the bill is built from). The goods-receipt "paid at delivery" shortcut calls findRule and is refused when a rule would hold the bill or its payment.
  • Add a document type: register an executor, add the type to SUPPORTED in the routes and to the table check, route its create endpoint through submitForApproval, add tests, update the BRD/SRS/manual.

11b. Tax engine (planned design, not built: Sprints 14 to 14f)

Target module map, so that every document follows one path (see the design note Taxation and tax profiles and decisions D18 to D24):

Module (planned) Responsibility
server/lib/tax/determine.ts From the transaction type, party profile, item class, place and date, returns the tax code per line through ordered rules; refuses a line with no rule; applies an authorised override
server/lib/tax/compute.ts Base, rate valid on the document date, tax-inclusive extraction, per-line rounding; the only place tax arithmetic lives (replaces the arithmetic in computeInvoiceServerTotals and the expense helper)
server/lib/tax/post.ts Turns tax lines into ledger lines through postJournalEntry: accounts of the code, recoverable and non-recoverable split, tags (code, component, direction, return box)
server/lib/tax/recovery.ts Category blocks and the apportionment percentage from the ledger
server/lib/tax/reverseCharge.ts, withholding.ts Imported services and payments to non-residents
server/lib/currency.ts, landedCost.ts, server/routes/customsEntries.ts Foreign currency, landed cost, customs entry (prerequisites of the import of goods)
server/routes/taxSetup.ts Codes, rates, rules, profiles; permissions and audit
server/lib/vatReturn.ts (changed) Return and registers from the ledger tags; settlement entry on filing

Existing code that must change so the new path is the only one: the invoice, quotation, credit note, POS, expense, purchase bill, goods receipt and return routes stop computing and posting tax themselves; the VAT return stops summing documents; the ZATCA XML builder reads category and reason from the tax code; the slab column becomes read-only after migration.

Traps to design against: a migration must not touch filed quarters or closed months; the ZATCA XML of existing documents must stay byte-identical (dual-gate check); a rate change must not alter issued documents; during the migration the slab and the tax code must never both drive one document.

12. Consistency checks (GET /api/reports/consistency-check)

R1.1 balance sheet balances · R1.2 trial balance balances · R2.x balances tie to registers · R3 stock register = quantity · R4 locked periods never move · R5.1 balance sheet balances at every earlier month end · R6.1 ledger agrees with documents per account (AR/AP net of money on account) · R7 journal soundness · R8.1–R8.4 party tags and subledger ties. Error-severity failures fail the test suites that call it.

13. Print

DocumentRenderer document types SettlementVoucher, JournalVoucher, BankReconciliation take server-resolved data (GET /settlements/:id, …/manual-journals/:id/print-data, …/journals/entries/:id/print-data, GET /bank-reconciliations/:id). Accounting documents follow the viewer's language and direction (isAccountingDoc). The overlay uses z-[60] so it sits above drawers.

14. Schema, RLS and release mechanics

  • src/db/schema.ts is the single source. npm run db:push diffs the live database; read the printed plan. Never accept a TRUNCATE prompt.
  • Prerequisite unique keys for composite foreign keys on populated tables are created by scripts/pre-push-keys.mjs (branches, customers, vendors, vouchers).
  • drizzle-kit drops RLS predicates and does not update changed CHECKs: always run npx tsx scripts/apply-rls-policies.mjs and npx tsx scripts/apply-check-constraints.mjs after a push.
  • Back-fill of party tags: scripts/backfill-party-tags.ts --apply (idempotent).
  • Translations: add rows to src/glTranslations.ts, then npx tsx scripts/apply-gl-translations.ts --apply (inserts missing, fills blanks, never overwrites edits).

15. Known traps (learned the hard way)

  • Dev server on 3001 has no watch: restart after backend edits. Tests: TEST_BASE_URL=http://localhost:3001.
  • Raw tx.execute returns DATE as string; drizzle select overrides parsers.
  • recordAuditLog sets req._auditLogged; use a stand-in req when calling outside a request.
  • A first release after changing release-demo.sh runs the old copy mid-pull: rerun.
  • Blueprint requirement ids in older code comments (FR-AR-01 / FR-AP-03 for allocation) are off by one; the correct ones are FR-AR-02 and FR-AP-04 (see BRD section 9).

Chapter 10

Accounting and reporting rules

Source: docs/design-notes/accounting-rules.md

These are the rules the system must obey. Every report is derived from the documents by these rules; GET /api/reports/consistency-check (server/lib/consistencyCheck.ts) tests them for any company at any time. When a figure ever disagrees with another, find which rule is violated and fix the rule's enforcement — never adjust the one figure.

A. Dates

  • A document that carries a user-chosen date (invoice, expense, purchase bill) must be dated in an existing, open fiscal month, not in the future, and outside any VAT quarter whose return is generated or filed.
  • Everything else is dated the day it is entered: goods receipt, stock movement, credit note, vendor return, payment, refund, reversal, stock adjustment, cancellation. A payment cannot be dated before the document it pays.
  • The stock register files each movement under the day it physically happened.

B. Locking

  • A closed month locks every document dated in it. A generated or filed VAT quarter locks every tax document dated in it (invoice, credit note, expense, bill, vendor return, and their cancellations).
  • A locked document is never changed, cancelled or dropped from its period. Reports for a locked period never move (closed months keep their stored profit; filed returns keep their figures).
  • A VAT return that is only generated freezes its quarter until it is deleted; a filed return is permanent.

C. Corrections

  • A document that is not locked may be cancelled (invoice not reported to ZATCA; expense; bill; return; GRN before billing). A cancelled expense posts its reversal on the day of cancellation.
  • A locked document is corrected only by a new document dated today, landing in the current open period and quarter:
    • sales invoice → credit note (its own date = issue date; the original invoice date stays on the original);
    • goods receipt / purchase bill → return to the vendor (reduces stock; reduces what is owed, or becomes a vendor credit receivable if the bill was already paid; VAT adjusted in the quarter of the return);
    • expense → reversal document (negative expense, same treatment as above);
    • money back from a vendor → vendor refund (clears the vendor credit).
  • A corrected-by-document original is never edited or removed; the correction is a separate dated event.

D. What each figure means

  • Revenue / COGS / expenses are by document date, tax-exclusive. Credit notes and reversals count negative in the period they are issued.
  • Stock is a Balance Sheet item: quantity on hand × average cost from goods receipts. A stock item sold before it is received simply carries cost 0 until stock arrives; a typed (non-stock) line never has a cost.
  • Accruals are estimates: owed at their net amount, no input VAT. The real invoice that settles one claims its own VAT, in its own period, and adds only the difference to cost.
  • VAT follows tax-invoice dates: output VAT = sales register, input VAT = purchase register (bills and expenses at original amounts, minus returns/reversals in their own periods).
  • As-at reports (Balance Sheet / Trial Balance for an earlier date) show what was owed and held on that date.

E. The checks (see consistencyCheck.ts)

R1 the books balance · R2 every balance ties to its register (bank, receivables, payables, inventory, VAT, profit) · R3 stock register = quantity on hand · R4 locked periods never move · R5 the books balance at every earlier month end · R6 the journal agrees with the documents.

Chapter 11

Period rule for cancel, reversal, return and credit

Source: docs/design-notes/reporting-rules.md

  1. A document can be cancelled, reversed or returned only while its own fiscal month is open and its VAT quarter is not filed. Otherwise the action is refused. While the period is open, treating the cancelled document as void in that period is correct, because nothing closed changes.
  2. A closed month or a filed VAT quarter is never touched and never corrected afterwards.
  3. A later correction is a NEW document dated today (credit note, purchase return, refund, supplier credit). It lands in the current open period and the current VAT quarter.
  4. Cancelling an expense posts its reversal on the day of cancellation.
  5. Every dated entry (bill, payment, refund, stock adjustment, cancel) must fall in an existing, open month and outside a filed quarter.

Guard: tests/closedPeriodImmutability.test.ts attempts every cancel, reversal, credit, return, payment, settlement and delete against a closed, filed period; each must be refused or leave the period unchanged, and the books must stay balanced.

Considered and not adopted: a cancelledAt column, retroactive VAT correction of a closed quarter.

Ledger-based statements: the existing document-based reports stay the figures of record. Ledger-based statements (General Ledger Step 1.5, docs/design-notes/gl-step1-5-decisions.md) run beside them and are cut over one at a time, as the Finance Core Blueprint's rollout step 4 prescribes, only after the two agree on every company.

Chapter 12

Date fields on documents: the standard (Demo / 2.0)

Source: docs/design-notes/date-fields-standard.md

Decided with the user (2026-10-15): every document table has the same core dates, named the same way, with the tier-1 ERP meaning (SAP, Oracle, Dynamics). This file is the rule every new table follows and the map of what each existing table has.

Naming

  • A business date (a calendar day, no time) is <meaning>_date, Postgres type date, in the code <meaning>Date as a YYYY-MM-DD string.
  • A system moment (set by the program, never typed) is <meaning>_at, type timestamp. Who did it is <meaning>_by_id.
  • One name, one meaning, on every table. Never date, dt, issued, txn_date and so on.

The core set

Field Meaning Who sets it Used for
doc_date The date of the document: the date printed on it (invoice date, bill date, order date). Every document table has it. The user (defaults to today) Document registers, sales / purchase reports, statements, numbering periods, aging basis
posting_date The date the ledger and the period locks use. Only on documents that post to the ledger or the stock ledger. Defaults to doc_date. The system (user only where a screen offers it, e.g. manual journals) The general ledger, every financial statement, open / closed month, VAT quarter, back-date rules
created_at, created_by_id When and who entered it. Never editable. System Audit: shows a document dated in the past was keyed later
updated_at, updated_by_id When and who last changed it. System Audit

Rule of use: financial statements and period locks read posting_date; operational reports read doc_date. Until a back-dating policy lets them differ, the two are equal. ZATCA: an invoice's issue date IS doc_date; posting_date of an invoice always equals it.

Other business dates (named once, reused everywhere)

due_date (payment due), payment_date (last payment received / made), supply_date (tax point / date of supply), valid_until_date (an offer's validity), required_by_date (needed by), delivery_date (promised delivery), expected_arrival_date, value_date (bank value date), cut_off_date (opening balances), received_date (physical receipt). A column with no screen and no rule behind it is not added yet: each is added with the feature that needs it, under this name.

Per table (all done 2026-10-15)

Table doc_date posting_date Other dates / audit
quotations yes - (posts nothing) valid_until_date, updated_at/by
invoices (incl. credit notes) yes (= the ZATCA issue date) yes (= doc_date) supply_date, due_date, payment_date, updated_at/by
expenses yes (the vendor bill's date) yes due_date, payment_date, updated_at/by
vouchers yes yes value_date
journal_entries (ledger header) yes (the source document's date) yes (the ledger date)
manual_journals yes yes (user may set it apart)
purchase_requisitions, purchase_orders yes - created_at/by, delivery_date (PO)
goods_receipt_notes, purchase_returns, physical_stock_takes yes yes created_at/by
purchase_bills yes (the supplier invoice date) yes due_date, created_at/by
warehouse_dispatches, warehouse_receivings yes yes created_at/by, expected_arrival_date (dispatch)
stock_ledger_transactions (a movement ledger) - yes created_at = the moment (orders movements within a day; average cost depends on it)

Known exceptions, to be converted with the feature that reworks them: payment_date on invoices and expenses is still a timestamp (AR / AP payment allocation), batch expiry_date columns are timestamps.

Which report reads which date: financial statements, bank ledger, period / back-date locks read posting_date; registers, customer / vendor statements, aging, VAT registers and quotation reports read doc_date. Report DTO rows keep a display field called date; table columns and API document rows carry the standard names.

Migration rule

The rename is done in place by scripts/migrate-date-fields.mjs (idempotent, run before db:push by both release scripts): ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN plus TYPE date USING, then the new columns are added and back-filled (posting_date = doc_date), so no row is lost and drizzle-kit never sees a drop. Tables that had a timestamp in date and no created_at get created_at back-filled from it, so ordering inside a day is not lost.

Chapter 13

Posting control: the lock date and back-dating rules (Demo / 2.0)

Source: docs/design-notes/posting-control.md

Built 2026-10-15 after the question "what is the rule for back-dating, and what do tier-1 ERPs do". Tier-1 systems (SAP posting periods, Oracle GL calendar, Dynamics ledger calendar) do not count "days back"; they control back-dating with period status, authorisation and audit. This is that, on top of what the system already had (open / closed fiscal month, VAT quarter freeze, ZATCA no-edit rule).

The rules, in the order they are checked (server/lib/postingGuard.ts, one function for every route)

  1. Fiscal month: the posting date must be in an existing, open month, not a future month. (Unchanged.)
  2. VAT quarter: a tax document may not be dated in a generated or filed quarter; any other posting may not be dated in a filed quarter. (Unchanged.)
  3. Lock date (posting_policies.lock_date): nothing may be dated on or before it, except by someone holding postingControl.overrideLock.
  4. Back-date window (max_back_days): nothing may be dated more than N days before today, except by someone holding postingControl.backDate. 0 = dated today or later.
  5. Reason (reason_after_days): a posting older than N days must carry a reason (backDateReason in the request; a manual journal keeps it on the journal so the approver does not retype it).
  6. A posting that used an override, or that carries a reason, is written to the audit log as BACKDATED_POSTING (route, posting date, days back, reason, which override).

With no policy row (a company that never opens the screen) only rules 1 and 2 apply, exactly as before. The administrator holds every right.

What is covered

Every route that posts something dated, or reverses something at its own date, calls the guard: invoices (create, credit note, cancel, quotation conversion, payment), POS returns, expenses (create, pay, reverse, cancel, recurring and accrual settlement), purchase bills (create, pay, cancel), purchase returns and cancellations, GRN reversal, vendor refunds (and their cancellation), stock adjustments and stock-take finalisation, inter-bank transfers, investor contributions, opening balances (post and reverse), and manual journals (at submit and again at approval). A quotation posts nothing, so it keeps the open-month rule only.

The date the rules judge is the posting date. A manual journal may have a posting date apart from its document date: a journal dated in a locked period but posted today is allowed; the reverse is refused.

Permissions (Roles screen, General Ledger group "Posting Control")

read (see the screen), update (change the policy), backDate (post beyond the window), overrideLock (post on or before the lock date). Everyone signed in can read the policy, so forms can ask for a reason.

Not built yet (from the tier-1 comparison, the next step of period control)

  • a restricted month status between Open and Closed that only named users can post into, and the adjustment period after year-end (fiscal_months.is_adjustment_period, Blueprint FR-YEC);
  • per-module close (AR, AP, inventory, GL closing on their own dates);
  • an approval route for a back-dated posting above an amount (Blueprint FR-APR).

Tests

tests/postingControl.test.ts (8): policy permissions and validation, no policy = no change, the window and its override with audit, the same rule on expenses and journals, the lock date including the cancellation of an old invoice, the reason rule from draft to approval, and a posting date apart from the document date.

Chapter 14

AR, AP and bank reconciliation: design against the Finance Core Blueprint (Demo / 2.0)

Source: docs/design-notes/ar-ap-bank-design.md · Decision record: written before building, with an as-built section

Written 2026-10-15 before building (architecture-first). Sources: docs/reference/Finance-Core-Blueprint.pdf (FR-AR-01..03, FR-AP-01..04, FR-BANK-01..05, data model pages 21-28), docs/design-notes/gl-step1-5-decisions.md (posting profiles, control accounts), the code as it stands.

What the code already has

  • Invoices, credit notes, expenses and purchase bills post to the ledger through postJournalEntry, and since slice 1b the receivable / payable line lands on the party's control account.
  • A payment is a voucher (Receipt / Payment / Reversal) plus a journal entry, created inline by about ten routes. A voucher points at ONE document (reference_type, reference_id). invoices.amount_paid / payment_status are kept by those routes.
  • Customer / vendor statements, "Outstanding Aging & Balances", the customer / vendor balance on a screen: all computed from the DOCUMENTS (invoices, vouchers ...), not from the ledger.
  • No bank reconciliation. No customer-tagged ledger line. No receipt applied across several invoices, no payment on account.

What the Blueprint requires (and the gap)

Requirement Gap
FR-AR-01 a posted invoice / credit note posts to the AR control account tagged with the customer the line is on the right account, but carries no customer: journal_lines.customer_id / vendor_id do not exist
FR-AR-03 aging and statements are a live query over posted journal lines on the control account, never a separate balance they are document-based
FR-AR-02 / FR-AP-04 a receipt / payment applies across one or more open invoices / bills, or is held on account, through a payment allocation record one voucher = one document; nothing on account
FR-AP-02 expenses and bills share one AP control account and vendor subledger true for the account; the vendor tag is missing, so one vendor position cannot be read from the ledger
FR-BANK-01..05 reconciliation session per bank per statement period, uncleared lines, live formula, complete only when it balances, discrepancy by journal entry, completed lines locked, reopening needs permission and is audited nothing
FR-JE-04 / FR-APR-01..05 approval rules per document type and amount not built (journals have maker-checker only)

Decisions

  1. The subledger is the ledger. journal_lines gain customer_id and vendor_id (typed, nullable, composite FK to the party within the company), set by the posting engine from the same party context that already picks the control account (server/lib/postingContext.ts): no route sets them. Existing lines are back-filled from their source document by an idempotent script. Whatever posts to a customer's receivable line carries that customer.
  2. Aging, ledger and statements are queries over those lines (server/lib/subledger.ts): a party's open balance is the sum of its lines on the control account(s); aging buckets by document due date with payments and credit notes matched oldest-first (the balance-forward method) until the allocation record (decision 4) exists, then by explicit allocation. Both agree to the cent by construction; a consistency rule proves control account = sum of party balances + unallocated.
  3. A consistency rule per control account joins the existing checks: the control account's ledger balance equals the sum of every party's balance, and no ledger line on a customer control account lacks a customer.
  4. Payment allocation (FR-AR-01, FR-AP-03): table payment_allocations (company, voucher, allocated-to type + id, amount, created at/by). A receipt or payment is still a voucher + journal entry; the allocation says which documents it settles. Every existing path that creates a Receipt / Payment voucher goes through one function (recordPaymentAllocations) so documents' amount_paid and the allocations cannot drift; a back-fill creates the allocations of history. New screens: Receive Payment (a customer, a bank, an amount, the open invoices to settle, the rest held on account) and Pay Vendor (the same for bills and expenses), plus apply credit note / on-account credit to an open invoice.
  5. Bank reconciliation exactly as FR-BANK-01..05: bank_reconciliations (bank, statement date, ending balance, GL balance at reconciliation, status, completed by / at) and bank_statement_lines (date, description, amount, matched ledger line); journal_lines gain bank_reconciliation_id and reconciled_at. A session lists the bank account's posted, uncleared lines; statement lines are typed in or imported (CSV / Excel) and matched by amount and date (suggested, never silent); completion is blocked unless Book balance + outstanding deposits - outstanding payments = statement balance; a bank charge or interest is posted as a journal entry from inside the session; a completed session locks its cleared lines; reopening needs a permission and is audited.
  6. Voucher and posting print-outs: the Receipt / Payment voucher print gains the posting (accounts debited and credited, with the party and the allocations); a new Journal Voucher print for a manual journal and for any posted ledger entry. Same print pipeline as every other document (templates, Arabic / RTL), no second renderer.
  7. Approval rules (FR-JE-04) stay for a later step; the guard in postingGuard.ts is where an approval check would hook in.

Build order (each step complete: schema, RLS and keys, engine, routes, permissions, screens, translations, tests, docs; deployed to the Demo)

A. party tags on ledger lines + back-fill + ledger-based customer / vendor ledger, aging and reconciliation rule (decisions 1-3) B. payment allocation, Receive Payment / Pay Vendor, credit application (decision 4) C. bank reconciliation (decision 5) D. voucher and journal-voucher print-outs (decision 6) E. the automation-ready test-case catalogue

As built (2026-10-15): where the build differs from, or settles, the design above

  • Allocation dates. payment_allocations also carries applied_date and reversed_date (business dates, not timestamps): "as at" reports must read the day the money was put against a document, not the day the row was typed. server/lib/settlementFacts.ts turns allocations back into the per-document vouchers the older reports expect, and is the single place that knows both shapes.
  • Money held on account is a liability (Customer advances) or an asset (Vendor advances) in the document-based Balance Sheet and Trial Balance, and is netted into AR / AP when the ledger is compared with the documents (the ledger has one receivable and one payable account; the documents show the advance as its own line).
  • Reversal vouchers and cash direction. One rule, reversalIsCashOut(referenceType) in financialReports.ts: a Reversal undoing a receipt (Invoice, VendorRefund, CustomerReceipt, BankInterest) is money out; undoing a payment is money in.
  • The reconciling formula is built the standard way: statement balance + deposits in transit − payments outstanding = book balance, i.e. cleared balance = statement balance. (The Blueprint prints the two outstanding terms with the opposite signs; the arithmetic of a real bank reconciliation is the one used.)
  • Bank fee / interest is not a free-form journal: money cannot leave a bank without a voucher in this application (every bank balance, the bank ledger and the Balance Sheet read vouchers), so the adjustment writes a voucher (BankCharge payment / BankInterest receipt) and the ledger entry, restricted to an expense account (charge) or an income account (interest). The bank-charge / interest figures are read from the ledger entry (as Cost of Goods Sold is) into the Profit & Loss, the month-close figure, the Trial Balance, the dashboard profit and the cash flow, so every document-based statement still balances.
  • Locking. "A completed reconciliation locks its cleared lines" is enforced where reversal happens: reverseAllEntriesFor (ledger.ts) refuses to reverse an entry that has a bank line cleared by a reconciliation. The cancel of a receipt / payment / invoice payment therefore fails as a whole (its transaction rolls back), with a message naming the way out (un-clear, or reopen).
  • Print model. Vouchers, journal vouchers and the reconciliation statement are printed from one server-resolved payload (server/lib/postingPrint.ts: entries with account, party and dimension labels), rendered by DocumentRenderer as three new internal document types that follow the viewer's language and direction.
  • Permissions follow the Blueprint list (bankReconciliation: create, read, match, complete, reopen) plus one added leaf, adjust, because posting an entry is a different authority from ticking lines.
    Note

    Note: Blueprint ids in this document were corrected on 2026-10-15 (allocation = FR-AR-02 / FR-AP-04, aging = FR-AR-03, invoice posting = FR-AR-01). The traceable register is docs/gl-ap-ar.

Chapter 15

GL Step 1.5: dimensions and configurable statements. CFO and architect decisions

Source: docs/design-notes/gl-step1-5-decisions.md · Decision record: written before building

Status: decided 2026-10-14, before any of this is built. Sources: docs/reference/Finance-Core-Blueprint.pdf (FR-DIM, FR-RPT-CFG, FR-RPT-GL, ERD, posting contract, rollout), docs/design-notes/accounting-rules.md, docs/design-notes/reporting-rules.md, docs/archive/gl-step1-plan.md, docs/archive/sprint-log-gl-step1.md, the project memory, and what the code does today. The earlier "statement layout designer" mock is superseded where it differs from the Blueprint (names and column model); its formatting, selector and safety ideas are kept.

What the documents already decided (and I must follow)

Requirement Blueprint decision Code today Gap
Dimensions FR-DIM-01: company-configurable dimension types, Branch and Department seeded, any number, each independently combinable on any line; one surrogate dimension_combination_id per line, find-or-create by dedup key Only journal_entries.branch_id; nothing else Whole framework
Cost centre cost_centers master, optional home branch, hierarchical; a Department dimension is linked to it none New
Reports FR-RPT-CFG: Trial Balance and P&L are two seeded, editable, cloneable instances of ONE Report Definition engine (report_definitions / report_rows / report_columns); rows select accounts by range, parent rollup or explicit list with include and exclude lists on top; balance display Debit/Credit/Net; indent; subtotal sums rows above; calculated rows by formula; columns = period basis x value basis x optional dimension filter; Summary or Detailed Two hardcoded ledger reports (account tree TB; GL) Engine, definitions, designer
Drill-down FR-RPT-GL-04: every figure clickable to General Ledger Detail (account set, period, dimension filter) GL takes one account Multi-account + dimension filter
Branch / comparative "Branch P&L Comparison, Comparative P&L: existing, via configurable columns" none Falls out of the column model
Posting postJournalEntry resolves each line's dimension tags to a combination, never writes lines elsewhere Stamps account_id only Add dimension tags
Cash flow, equity changes FR-RPT-STMT-01/02: derived statements none Fixed format (user: "ok until requirements appear")
Year-end close FR-CLOSE: fiscal years, one closing entry into Retained Earnings none Later step; Balance Sheet "profit to date" is cumulative until then
Not now Bank reconciliation, cost distribution (allocation) runs, fixed assets, landed cost, approval rules by amount none Later; this design must not block them

CFO decisions (what the books must guarantee)

  1. One ledger, one engine. Every statement is a query over posted journal lines. The new engine produces the Trial Balance, Income Statement and Balance Sheet; no second calculation is added next to it.
  2. Existing document-based reports are not changed and keep being the figures of record until a cut-over is decided. Blueprint rollout step 4 (cut reports over one at a time, old and new side by side) is followed: Ledger vs Documents stays and gains the new statements' totals. (This supersedes the line "ledger-based reports considered and not adopted" in reporting-rules.md; it is updated.)
  3. Integrity shown on the page: a Balance Sheet says in words whether Assets = Liabilities + Equity; a Trial Balance says whether debits = credits; any account with a balance that no row covers is listed as "Not in this definition" with a warning, so money can never go missing silently.
  4. No double counting: one account may be selected by only one Accounts row of a definition (after include/exclude), refused on save.
  5. Dimensions are analytical, not a second ledger: they never change an account balance. P&L accounts normally carry them; a balance sheet filtered by a dimension is shown with a notice that it is partial.
  6. Profit to date: until Year-End Close exists, "Profit (loss) to date" on the Balance Sheet is the cumulative result of Revenue and Expense accounts. Year-End Close is the next GL step (Blueprint FR-CLOSE) and will take over this row.
  7. Controls: layout design, dimension setup and viewing are separate permissions; every change to a definition, dimension or rule is audit-logged; posted figures never change because a definition changed (definitions only change presentation).
  8. Dimension requirement policy: an account can mark a dimension Required, Optional or Not allowed (default value optional). Manual journals enforce Required strictly. Documents (invoice, expense, bill...) apply defaults but do not enforce Required until their forms collect dimensions (next slice); otherwise turning a rule on would stop invoices being issued.

Architect decisions

  1. Names follow the Blueprint: dimension_types, dimension_values, dimension_combinations, dimension_combination_entries, cost_centers, report_definitions, report_rows, report_columns (plus report_row_selectors, and account_dimension_rules). The unpushed statement_* tables are removed.
  2. Dimension storage: journal_lines.dimension_combination_id (and the same on manual_journal_lines) with composite (company_id, id) foreign keys. A combination is deduplicated by a hash of its sorted type=entity members, so a filter is one indexed lookup, not a per-line join.
  3. No polymorphic ids without foreign keys. An entry stores its entity in a typed column that has a real foreign key (value_id to dimension_values, branch_id to branches, cost_center_id to cost_centers), plus a denormalised entity_id for fast filtering, with a check that they agree. branches gains a (company_id, id) unique key so the link cannot cross companies.
  4. Branch has one meaning: journal_entries.branch_id stays (branch visibility and existing reports use it). The Branch dimension of a line is its combination's branch if it has one (allocation entries, later), otherwise the entry's branch. No backfill is needed.
  5. One shared query layer: resolvedLines (already the single definition of "which account does a line belong to") gains the dimension filter; General Ledger, journal listing, Trial Balance and every report use it. Descendants of a hierarchical value are expanded once, in one helper.
  6. One engine (server/lib/reportEngine.ts): rows x columns evaluated from per-account debit/credit totals fetched once per column; the existing /reports/trial-balance route becomes an adapter over the seeded Trial Balance definition (same response shape, so the screen and tests keep working); /reports/income-statement, /reports/balance-sheet and custom definitions use the same run function.
  7. Columns: periodBasis (CurrentPeriod, YearToDate, PriorPeriod, PriorYear, AsAtEnd, CustomRange) x valueBasis (Debit, Credit, Net, Movement, OpeningBalance, ClosingBalance), presentation single or Dr/Cr pair, optional dimension filter, plus variance and percent columns that refer to other columns.
  8. Rows: Heading, Accounts (selectors: account, parent rollup, range, type, posting role; each include or exclude), Subtotal (sum of rows above since the previous subtotal), Calculated (formula over row numbers), Text, Spacer, ProfitToDate. Value rule per Accounts row: Net, Debit, Credit, Debit balances only, Credit balances only, Split (no netting across accounts); optional opposite sign; show always / only when not zero / hidden. Formatting (bold, italic, underline, indent, alignment of label and amount, borders, shading, negatives style, page break) lives in one format object validated by one shared module used by the server and the screens.
  9. Drill-down contract: every evaluated cell returns { accountIds, from, to, dims, basis }, and General Ledger Detail accepts exactly that.
  10. Excel export carries the formatting (a styled-workbook library replaces the style-less one for these exports); the printed page renders the same table.
  11. Permissions: dimensions (create, read, update, delete=deactivate), reportDefinitions (create, read, update, delete=deactivate), and report leaves incomeStatementLedger, balanceSheetLedger, cashFlowLedger, equityChangesLedger, customLedgerReports next to the existing trialBalanceLedger and generalLedger.
  12. Everything an addition must touch (deletion routine, test teardown, RLS and grants, seed script for existing companies, consistency rules, EN/AR/UR text, release script, docs) is part of each slice, not a follow-up.

Build order (each slice complete: schema, RLS and keys, engine, routes, permissions, screens, translations, tests, docs)

  1. Dimension framework: types, values, cost centres, combinations, account rules, posting-engine tagging, manual journal pickers, dimension filters in General Ledger / journal listing / reports, setup screens.
  2. Report definition engine: tables, engine, seeded Trial Balance / Income Statement / Balance Sheet, adapters, designer, statement screens with comparative and dimension columns, drill-down, print and styled Excel.
  3. Cash Flow and Changes in Equity (fixed format).
  4. Demo release, sprint log, then AR and AP (new analysis against the Blueprint first), then the automation-ready test-case catalogue.

Known deviations from the Blueprint (deliberate)

  • Approval is the simple maker-checker already built; amount-bracket approval rules stay a later step.
  • Dimension rules per account (account_dimension_rules) are an addition that implements FR-JE-03 "lines carry the dimensions relevant to their account".
  • Row dimension filters are stored in the row (like column filters in the Blueprint) rather than in a child table; dimension values are only ever deactivated, so a stored filter cannot dangle.

Control accounts and posting profiles (decided after the user asked: "select a party or item in any transaction and the rules apply automatically")

What the documents say: control accounts are accounts only a subledger may post to (FR-COA-02, glossary); a line carries the customer / vendor / tax tag as a strongly typed field (FR-JE-03, ERD); bank_accounts gain a linked GL account, tax_slabs gain Output/Input VAT account links, product categories replace the free-text glGroupMappings with real account keys (ERD "Modified" list); default dimensions flow from the context to the line (Dynamics model the user chose). What the code does: a document names a posting ROLE (AR, AP, INVENTORY, SALES_REVENUE, COGS ...) and postJournalEntry stamps the company's current account for that role; banks and expense categories already resolve to their own account; customers, vendors, products and tax slabs have no account or dimension defaults.

Decision: one resolution step, inside the posting engine, driven by a posting context. A document does not pick accounts; it passes WHO and WHAT it concerns, and the engine resolves accounts and dimension tags.

  1. company_accounts gains subledger_type (customer, vendor, inventory, bank, tax, asset, none). A control account is an account with a subledger type; only that subledger's documents post to it (manual journals keep refusing them). Several control accounts per type are allowed (Trade receivables, Staff receivables); the role account (AR, AP ...) remains the company default.
  2. Master records may point at their control account (customers.control_account_id, vendors.control_account_id; only a control account of the matching subledger type is accepted), and at default dimensions (default_dimension_combination_id, a combination exactly as on a ledger line) on customers, vendors, products/categories, banks. Tax slabs get their Output/Input VAT accounts; product categories get inventory / sales / COGS accounts (replacing the free-text GL groups).
  3. postJournalEntry takes an entry-level context ({ customerId, vendorId, branchId, bankId, productCategoryId, taxSlabId }). Role lines resolve first through the context (customer's control account, bank's own account, category account, slab account), then through Account Mapping (today's behaviour); dimension tags merge in this priority: line-specific, product/category, customer/vendor, bank, branch, account default; then the account rules decide what is allowed or required.
  4. Because every document already calls postJournalEntry, existing routes only gain a context argument; no route re-implements account selection.
  5. The strongly typed customer_id / vendor_id tags on journal_lines arrive with AR/AP (so aging becomes a query over control-account lines), and a consistency rule checks every control account against the sum of its subledger.
  6. Order: dimension framework core (in progress) -> posting context and master defaults (slice 1b) -> report engine -> AR -> AP. Product-category revenue/COGS splitting needs line-level product context and is part of slice 1b only for accounts the documents already know (customer, vendor, bank, branch, tax slab); per-category revenue lines follow with AR.

Chapter 16

Sprint 11 — Approval rules: what tier-1 ERPs do, and what we should build

Source: docs/design-notes/approval-rules-tier1-research.md · Decision record: Sprint 11 research and owner decisions

Written 2026-10-15 for BR-JNL-10 (Blueprint FR-APR-01..06). Based on the documented behaviour of SAP S/4HANA, Oracle Fusion / NetSuite, Microsoft Dynamics 365 Finance and Odoo as I know them; not re-verified against current vendor documentation, so confirm details before quoting them externally.

1. How the four compare

Aspect SAP S/4HANA Oracle Fusion (and NetSuite) Dynamics 365 Finance Odoo / mid-market
Mechanism Release strategies (purchasing), Flexible Workflow (finance: journal, payment, bank), parked documents Approval Management Engine / BPM rules; NetSuite SuiteFlow + approval routing Workflow editor: conditions, tasks, approval steps, escalation Approval rules per model (some editions)
What triggers it Document type + amount + characteristics (company code, cost centre, plant) Document type, amount, supplier, department, requester Document type + conditions on any field (amount, legal entity, vendor group) Amount threshold per document type
Levels Sequential or parallel release codes; up to 8 levels (purchasing) Approval chain by hierarchy (manager chain) or fixed list; parallel/serial Multi-step, sequential, parallel, "any one / all" One or two levels
Approver resolution Release code → user via authorisation; org-structure, rules Supervisor hierarchy, job level, position, user list, group User, role, position hierarchy, "manager of submitter", queues User or group
Self-approval Prevented by separation of duties (SoD) rules and "no approve own" setting Prevented by default (submitter skipped in chain) Option "submitter cannot approve"; SoD rules Configurable
Delegation / absence Substitutes, "workflow substitutions" with validity dates Delegation rules with dates; reassign Delegation, out-of-office, reassignment Limited
Escalation / timeout Deadline monitoring, escalate to superior Escalate after N days, auto-approve or reject options Timeouts with escalation Rare
What is held Parked/blocked document; nothing posts until released; payment run excludes blocked items Document "Pending Approval", no accounting until approved Document "In review"/"Submitted"; posting blocked Draft
Rejection Back to originator with comment; can be recalled Rejected → originator edits and resubmits; reject reasons Reject/return/request change with comment Back to draft
Audit trail Change documents + workflow log (who, when, decision, comment) Approval history on the document; full audit Workflow history, immutable Chatter log
Amount change after approval Release resets if value rises beyond a tolerance Re-approval if amount changes Document locked while in workflow; edit recalls it Usually re-approve
Payments Payment approval via bank-communication workflow; dual control on payment files (four-eyes) Payment batch approval; bank-file release Payment journals have their own workflow; positive pay Usually none

2. Principles every tier-1 system shares (these are the requirements, regardless of vendor)

  1. Rule-driven, not blanket. Only documents matching a rule enter approval; everything else posts directly (our FR-APR-06). Approval is overhead on high-value or sensitive items.
  2. Nothing hits the ledger (or releases funds) until approved. The document sits in a pre-posted state (parked / pending); reports and subledgers never see it.
  3. Separation of duties enforced by the server. The submitter can never approve their own item at any level; a second control is that approvers cannot approve beyond their own authority limit.
  4. Authority by amount band and by role, not by named person alone; approvers are resolved from roles / hierarchy so staff changes do not break rules.
  5. Sequential levels above higher thresholds, with parallel/any-of options at one level.
  6. Immutable history: who, when, level, decision, remarks; visible on the document.
  7. Changing a pending document invalidates approvals (edit = recall and resubmit, or re-approve if the amount rises).
  8. Delegation and escalation for absence and delay (substitute with validity dates; escalate after a timeout).
  9. Rejection returns to the submitter with remarks; nothing rejected ever posts.
  10. Payments are the strictest case: dual control, and blocked payments are excluded from payment runs.

3. What we already have (do not rebuild)

  • Manual journals: Draft → Pending → Posted with maker-checker, rejection with reason, optional self-approval switch, event history (manual_journals, manual_journal_events). This is a single-level, always-on rule hard-wired into the journal route.
  • Posting guard, audit log, permission registry, per-document posting paths.
  • Documents that post immediately today: vendor bills, expenses, receipts, vendor payments, credit notes.

4. Recommended design for Sprint 11 (fits our architecture)

One generic approval engine, not one workflow per document. The manual-journal maker-checker becomes the first client of the engine; bills, payments and credit notes follow.

4.1 Data (all company-scoped, RLS, composite keys)

  • approval_rules — company, document type (Journal, VendorBill, VendorPayment, CreditNote, later Receipt, Expense), name, active, amount from / to, optional conditions (branch, vendor, currency-free), priority, effective dates.
  • approval_rule_levels — rule, level number, approver type (Role | User | Permission), approver id, mode (Any one | All), optional amount ceiling per level, escalation days.
  • approval_requests — one per submitted document: document type + id, rule used (snapshot of the levels at submit time), status (Pending, Approved, Rejected, Recalled), current level, amount at submit, submitter, timestamps.
  • approval_actions — request, level, actor, action (Approved, Rejected, Delegated, Escalated, Recalled), remarks, time. Append-only.
  • approval_delegations — delegator, delegate, document types, valid from/to.

4.2 Engine behaviour

  1. A document route calls submitForApproval(type, id, amount) instead of posting. The engine finds the matching active rule (lowest priority number wins; band by amount); no match → returns "post directly" (FR-APR-06).
  2. A match creates the request, sets the document status to Pending Approval and posts nothing. Vendor payment: no voucher, no bank movement, excluded from any payment run.
  3. decide(requestId, level, action, remarks) is the only way forward: checks the caller holds the level's authority (role/permission/delegation), is not the submitter (FR-APR-03, server-side), and has not already acted at an earlier level.
  4. Last level approved → the engine calls the document's own post function inside the same transaction; the posting guard runs again at that moment (as manual journals do today).
  5. Reject → document returns to Draft with remarks; edit of a pending document recalls the request; an amount increase restarts approval.
  6. Everything is audited and shown in the document's history; a worklist screen ("My approvals", with counts in the menu) lists what waits for the signed-in user.

4.3 Screens

Approval Rules (list, rule editor with levels and amount bands, test-a-document preview), My Approvals (worklist, bulk approve for same-rule items), history panel on every document, delegation settings.

4.4 Permissions

approvalRules (create, read, update, delete); approvals.decide is not a global right: authority comes from the rule's level (role/permission), so a person can approve vendor payments up to a band without being able to approve journals. Optional approvals.override for an owner emergency approval, audited.

4.5 Rollout safety

  • Default = no rules = today's behaviour exactly (journals keep their single-level flow until an owner defines a rule, then the engine takes over).
  • Migrate journals last, after bills and payments prove the engine.
  • Tests: threshold boundary (equal to / just above), two-level chain, submitter never approves, same person cannot approve two levels, delegation window, amount change restart, rejection path, posting guard re-check at approval, payment excluded until approved, cross-company isolation.

5. Decisions needed from the owner before building

  1. Which documents first? Recommended: vendor payments and vendor bills (money out), then credit notes, then fold manual journals into the engine.
  2. Approver model: roles only (simplest), or roles plus the user's manager hierarchy? Our users have roles but no manager hierarchy today; recommended roles + named users in v1.
  3. Self-approval: keep today's company-level switch for journals, or forbid absolutely for payments (recommended: absolute for payments).
  4. Currency of thresholds: company currency only (single-currency scope).
  5. Delegation and escalation in v1? Recommended: delegation yes (absence is real), escalation to v1.1.
  6. Approval by email/mobile? Recommended: not in v1 (in-app worklist and notification count only).
  7. Should reopening a bank reconciliation, overriding the lock date or back-dating also route through approval? Tier-1 systems usually keep these as authority rights, not workflow; recommended: keep as rights.

6. Out of scope for S11

Parallel multi-branch routing by cost-centre hierarchy, e-signature, scheduled auto-approve, approval by external email links.

7. Owner decisions (2026-10-15) — binding for Sprint 11

  1. Order: vendor payments and vendor bills first, then credit notes, then manual journals folded into the engine.
  2. Approvers: roles and the manager hierarchy. Users gain a manager link (users.manager_id, additive, nullable) and a rule level may name "manager of the submitter" (and the manager's manager, up to N steps) besides a role, a user or a permission.
  3. Self-approval: a company-level setting in General Ledger settings, on or off. Off (the default) = a submitter can never approve their own document at any level; on = allowed, the document is flagged "self-approved" and the setting change and every use are audited. The setting covers payments, bills and credit notes as well as journals (today's journal switch becomes this one setting).
  4. Delegation with validity dates in version 1; escalation after a timeout in 1.1.
  5. Notification inside the system only: a "My approvals" worklist and a count in the menu. No email or mobile.
  6. Back-dating, lock-date override and reopening a reconciliation remain authority rights, not workflow.

Chapter 17

Agreed decisions register (finance core and the way we work)

Source: docs/design-notes/agreed-decisions-register.md · Standing register: read before any finance design

Purpose. One list of what the owner and the team have agreed, so that no new analysis, design or build contradicts it, and so that nothing agreed is forgotten when the work moves to another area. It exists because a benchmark written on 2026-10-19 proposed separate profit-centre and project modules, which contradicted the agreed design that dimensions are unlimited and flexible. The correction is recorded here as AG-11.

Rules for using it.

  1. Read it all before working on the chart of accounts, dimensions, posting, account determination, tax, budgeting, costing, periods, reports or any finance design. Start the work by listing the entries that apply (AG numbers) and say whether the new work follows each one.
  2. A new proposal that conflicts with an entry must say so in its first paragraph and ask the owner; it may not quietly replace it.
  3. Entries marked "owner-stated" are the owner's words or explicit instructions and outrank any document.
  4. After any decision, add or change the entry in the same session (with date and source). Proposals that are not yet agreed go in the last section, not above.
  5. The skill .claude/skills/agreed-decisions/SKILL.md and CLAUDE.md make this binding for every session.
  6. Agreed does not mean frozen: compare the applicable entries with tier-1 practice and the professional checklist (sections J and K) and propose changes (AG-77).

Status: Built (in the code), Planned (agreed, in the sprint plan), Standing (a way of working).

A. Ledger, chart of accounts and posting

AG-01 One ledger, one engine. Every statement is a query over posted journal lines; no second calculation CFO decision 1 (gl-step1-5-decisions) Built
AG-02 Company-owned chart with 16 posting roles that documents use; re-pointing a role is not retroactive Solution design D1 Built
AG-03 Control accounts by subledger type; only their own documents post to them; several per type are allowed; customers and vendors may name their own control account D2; posting profiles decision Built
AG-04 Documents name who and what, never accounts. One resolution step inside the posting engine, driven by a posting context (party, item, branch, bank, category), finds accounts and dimension tags. AG-10 Dimensions are unlimited and configurable. A company defines any number of dimension types (Branch and Department are seeded); each is combinable on any ledger line; values form hierarchies. They are the single mechanism for budgeting, costing, control and profit and loss by any analytical view. Owner-stated, reaffirmed 2026-10-19 Blueprint FR-DIM-01; owner Built (types, values, combinations, rules, filters)

Profit centre, cost centre, project, product line, region and channel are dimension types. AG-18 The GL core is domain-neutral and flexible: its structure must be able to carry the scenarios of any domain (retail, trading, manufacturing including order costing, real estate, contracting, services, fund accounting) if and when asked, without redesign. Domain modules are built only on request and use the core's general primitives; the core holds no domain-specific tables. Analysis is not a request to build. Owner-stated 2026-10-19 Owner Standing; structural readiness reviewed in domain-neutral-core
AG-19 Tax is a layered structure built on tax profiles. Tax profiles are attached to customers, vendors, items and the company; the system determines the tax treatment and the accounts from the profiles and the transaction type; the data entry operator selects neither the tax nor any account; the structure is flexible for every scenario (retail, trading, imports, manufacturing, real estate) and follows the best financial standards. Owner-stated 2026-10-19. The direction is agreed; the detailed design (taxation-analysis) is a proposal awaiting the decisions in its section 10. Today's single VAT output and input accounts are an interim state Owner Direction agreed (Standing); design Planned (Sprints 14 to 14f); interim behaviour Built
AG-20 Build the standard, tier-1-compliant solution including optional capabilities, with no patches or workarounds; flexible standard accounting. Owner 2026-10-19, in reply to the pending-decisions list Owner Standing
AG-21 Tax details accepted: Saudi VAT first, built jurisdiction-aware; components in the order all VAT scenarios, import VAT with duty and reverse charge, withholding, excise; import VAT supports both treatments per company (tax adviser to confirm); partial recovery designed in; tax-inclusive or exclusive as a company setting with an item override; override only with the right and a reason, audited; return boxes supplied by the tax adviser; each existing slab becomes a tax code Owner 2026-10-19 Planned (Sprints 14 to 14f)
AG-22 Core foundations accepted: an accounting-events layer with preview and re-derivation; parallel ledgers and currency (designed now, built early); typed business-object references; quantity and unit on ledger lines; one recognition-schedule engine; one account-determination engine; segregation of duties and master-data approval. Order: events and currency first Owner 2026-10-19 Planned (F1 to F5, F8, F9)
AG-23 Chart and dimensions accepted: account master and templates first, then dimension capabilities and budgeting; profit centre is a balancing dimension type (the branch can be that type by default); project costing: cost collection and profitability first, billing and recognition later; budget control configurable, warn by default; chart templates for retail, trading, manufacturing, real estate and services Owner 2026-10-19 Planned (F4, F6, F7)
AG-24 Reference standard: IFRS as adopted in Saudi Arabia, with local statutory and management views mappable as chart views; to be confirmed by the company's accountant Owner 2026-10-19 Planned (F4)

F. Approvals

ID Agreed Source Status
AG-50 Maker-checker for manual journals first; amount-threshold rules layered on top (a matching rule replaces the single step) D14 Built
AG-51 An approval request holds the intent of a document, never a half-posted document; the document is created by its own executor at the last approval; a shortcut cannot bypass a rule D15, D16 Built
AG-52 Approvers by role, named user, permission or manager of the submitter; delegation with dates in version 1 (all level types); escalation later; notifications in the system only; one company setting decides self-approval Sprint 11 owner decisions Built (escalation Planned)

G. Tenancy, security and environments

ID Agreed Source Status
AG-60 Every business table carries a company; the company of a request comes only from the authenticated request; every table has a row-level-security policy; company-scoped links use composite keys Platform architecture Standing
AG-61 Permissions are the union of the user's roles, with create / read / update / delete and a few action leaves; record state is checked separately Permission model Standing
AG-62 Live (1.0) takes fixes only; all new development is on the 2.0 branch and Demo; nothing from 2.0 reaches Live until the owner decides Environments Standing

The Blueprint is not the ceiling. Owner 2026-10-19 Owner Standing

J. Professional review of the agreed decisions (tier-1 practice and accounting guidelines)

Agreed does not mean frozen (AG-77). Each agreed entry is compared with how tier-1 systems work and with professional accounting practice, and the result is one of: Confirmed (consistent), Refine (keep, with an addition), Challenge (the wording or the substance may be wrong and needs a decision). Nothing below changes an entry until the owner agrees; the suggestions are the proposals. This review is professional judgement from general knowledge of SAP, Oracle, Dynamics 365, NetSuite and the IFRS framework; the standards were not re-read in this session, so specific clauses should be confirmed by the company's accountant.

AG Verdict Comparison and suggested change
01 One ledger, one engine Confirmed SAP's universal journal and Dynamics 365's general ledger work this way. Refine: add the capability for parallel ledgers (local standard, IFRS, tax or zakat books) and a subledger accounting layer (Oracle) so the same document can produce more than one accounting view; keep one engine.
02 Posting roles Refine Tier-1 systems find accounts by role and by item or product class, party class, branch and transaction type (SAP account determination, Oracle subledger accounting rules). Suggest: roles remain the default, with determination rules on top (the same mechanism as tax determination). Non-retroactive mapping is correct practice.
03 Control accounts Confirmed Matches reconciliation accounts in SAP and control accounts in Oracle and D365. Refine: allow a controlled, audited manual adjustment to a control account that still carries the party tag (provisions, write-offs), instead of refusing every manual posting.
04 Documents name who and what Confirmed This is exactly tier-1 account determination. Extend it to item class and transaction type (see AG-02).
05 Typed party tags Refine Add the other subledger references tier-1 systems carry: item (inventory), employee, fixed asset, project, tax code. One typed-reference mechanism, not one column per subledger.
06 Chart handling Refine Add "blocked for posting" separate from inactive, effective dating, approval of chart changes, and the account attributes in the chart benchmark (class, cash-flow class, tax category, currency).
07 Balanced entries Refine Keep. Add: for a balancing dimension type, the entry must balance per value (inter-unit clearing), as Oracle's balancing segment and SAP's profit-centre accounting do.
10 Dimensions unlimited Confirmed D365 financial dimensions are the closest model; Oracle and SAP have fixed segment sets, so ours is more flexible. Refine: "unlimited" needs a stated performance envelope (combinations, report scans) measured with a large data set.
11 Management accounting as dimension capabilities Confirmed, with a wording refinement D365 runs projects and cost centres on dimensions too; SAP and Oracle use dedicated structures. The agreed principle (no separate ledger) is sound. Refine the wording: processes that need documents (project billing, work in progress, revenue recognition, budget commitments) are process modules keyed by a dimension value, and are allowed; what is forbidden is a second ledger or a hard-coded object that bypasses the framework.
12 Dimensions are analytical Challenge True for the account total, but profit-centre accounting in SAP and balancing segments in Oracle produce a balance sheet and profit and loss per unit. Suggest rewording: "A dimension never changes an account's total. A type flagged balancing must also balance per value."
13 Combination storage Confirmed Deduplicated combinations are what D365 does. Test filtering at scale.
14 Rules per account Refine Tier-1 also has conditional rules (required for certain document types, origins or amounts; D365 advanced rule structures). Suggest as a later extension.
15 Default priority Confirmed
16 Shared query layer Confirmed
17 Allocations Refine SAP assessments and distributions, Oracle allocations and D365 allocation rules support several bases including statistical drivers and any dimension. Widen as proposed (BR-DIM-07).
20 One report engine Refine Add report versions and comparatives under governance, consolidation, statutory formats and a machine-readable export (XBRL-ready) where required.
21 Integrity on the page Confirmed A strong internal-control feature; keep.
22 No double counting Refine Presentation standards (IAS 1) forbid offsetting and require splitting an account by balance side in some statements (an overdrawn bank is a liability). Add a "split by balance sign" row type so a rule against double counting does not prevent correct presentation.
23 Drill-down Confirmed
24 Side-by-side reports Confirmed, needs an end date Tier-1 practice is one source. Keep the gate, but set a decision date and criteria for the cut-over so two sets of figures do not live indefinitely.
25 Server-side figures Confirmed
26 Permissions and audit Refine Add segregation-of-duties conflict rules (for example create and approve a vendor payment, create vendor and change its bank account), field-level and period-based rights, and change history on master data (internal-control frameworks such as COSO, SOX-style controls).
30 Dates Refine Add the dates the tax and currency work needs: tax point (time of supply), value date, exchange-rate date. The standard already allows them.
31 Posting guard Confirmed Tier-1 has period status per module (general ledger, receivables, payables, inventory) and adjustment periods; planned in Sprint 12.
32 Cancel only in open periods Confirmed Consistent with IAS 8: no retrospective rewriting of closed periods; correction by a later reversing entry.
33 Year-end close Refine Tier-1 systems differ: SAP carries balances forward without posting closing entries to profit and loss accounts; Dynamics posts closing transactions. Either is acceptable. Decide which one, and make sure year-to-date reports and carried-forward balances are consistent with the choice. Keep the rule that a closed year is not reopened.
40 Settlement model Confirmed Same as SAP clearing and Dynamics settlement. Refine: add payment terms, early-payment discounts, write-off and tolerance handling, payment runs.
41 to 43 Confirmed Internal consistency rules; nothing to change.
44 Credit and debit notes Confirmed Required by the e-invoicing regulation.
45 No hard delete Confirmed Audit-trail requirement. Refine: add a retention and anonymisation policy for personal data in master records (data-protection law), without breaking financial history.
46 No bulk save Confirmed
50 to 52 Approvals Refine Add approval of master-data changes (vendor bank account, credit limit, tax profile), parallel and conditional approval, and approval by account or cost object. Escalation is already planned.
60 Tenancy Confirmed
61 Permissions Refine See AG-26.
70 to 76 Ways of working Confirmed AG-71 is extended by AG-77.

Net result of the review. One entry should change in wording (AG-12) and one in scope (AG-11); the rest stand with additions. The additions cluster on the same foundations as before: an accounting-rules layer, parallel ledgers, currency, per-module periods, segregation of duties and master-data governance.
