K. Professional accounting checklist (applied to new designs and to this register)

Principle or standard The test for our design Where we stand
Double entry and completeness Every transaction has a balanced entry; nothing posts outside the engine Met (AG-07, AG-31)
Accrual and cut-off Revenue and cost in the right period; accruals and deferrals supported Partial: recurring accruals only; prepaid and deferred items absent
Immutability and audit trail A posted entry is never edited; correction by reversal; who, what and when kept Met for entries; master-data change history is partial
Authorisation and segregation of duties Approval rules; conflicting duties separated Approvals met; conflict rules absent
Sequential numbering Statutory documents numbered without duplicates, gaps explained Met for numbering; gap reporting absent
Reconciliation Subledger equals control account; bank reconciled Met
Period control Open, closed, locked periods; adjustments disclosed Met for months; year-end planned
Presentation (IAS 1, IAS 7) Current versus non-current, no inappropriate offsetting, cash flow statement Partial: no sub-classes; fixed-format cash flow
Inventory (IAS 2) Cost formulas, lower of cost and net realisable value, landed cost Partial: average cost only; no write-down; landed cost planned
Foreign currency (IAS 21) Functional currency, rates, revaluation, exchange differences Absent
Revenue (IFRS 15) Performance obligations, deferral, contract assets and liabilities Absent
Property, plant and equipment (IAS 16), leases (IFRS 16) Asset register, depreciation, disposal Absent (out of scope)
Provisions and credit losses (IAS 37, IFRS 9) Bad-debt provision, other provisions Absent
Taxes Indirect tax accounting, withholding, zakat and income tax feed Partial; design proposed
Local law (e-invoicing, VAT, records retention) Statutory formats and retention Met for e-invoicing and VAT filing locks; retention policy to define

Use this table as a checklist for every new finance design: for each row say whether the design meets it and, if not, which later sprint will.

Chapter 18

A domain-neutral GL core: structural readiness for retail, trading, manufacturing, real estate and any other domain

Source: docs/design-notes/domain-neutral-core.md · Structural readiness review: proposal for decision, nothing built

Written 2026-10-19 on the owner's instruction: "I did not ask you to proceed for work orders development, but the GL structure should be capable of handling all such scenarios if and when needed, order costing also. We need a flexible structure capable of doing all possible scenarios from domains like retail, real estate, manufacturing, trade."

This note proposes no domain module. It answers one question: is the GL core structured so that any domain's scenarios can be added later, as modules, without redesigning the core? Nothing here is a request to build work orders, projects, leases or anything else. Domain modules are built only when the owner asks.

Agreed entries that apply (register agreed-decisions-register): AG-01, AG-02, AG-04, AG-05 (follows); AG-10, AG-11, AG-12 to AG-17 (follows; AG-11 and AG-12 have proposed wording changes); AG-71 and AG-77 (this note is the review they require). Contradicts none. The owner statement above is recorded as AG-18.

Confidence. The primitives and domain scenarios come from general knowledge of tier-1 ERPs (SAP, Oracle, Dynamics 365, NetSuite) and IFRS (IAS 2, 16, 23, 40; IFRS 15, 16); they were not re-read against the standards in this session, and the illustrative traces are teaching examples, not accounting advice.

1. The principle

A flexible finance core is made of a small number of general primitives that every domain reuses, plus domain modules that sit on top and use only those primitives. The core never learns the words work order, lease, unit, shift or store. If a domain needs a new table, it is a domain table; if it needs a new kind of ledger information, that is a missing primitive and must be added to the core once, for everyone.

The test of a domain-neutral core: a new domain can be added by registering object types, dimension types, accounting events, determination rules, recognition schedules and runs, without changing the posting engine or the ledger tables.

2. The primitives the core must offer

# Primitive What it is Where we stand
P1 Account master Classification, posting controls, tax category, currency, groups, effective dating Basic; enrichment proposed (chart benchmark 7.1)
P2 Dimension framework Unlimited types with hierarchies, rules per account; roles, attributes, lifecycle, valid combinations, balancing Types, values, combinations, rules, filters built; the rest proposed
P3 Typed business-object references A line can reference any registered business object (party, item, asset, project, order, property, unit, contract, lease, fund) by a typed, validated key Customer and vendor only; a generic mechanism is missing
P4 Quantities and statistical values on lines Units, hours, area, occupancy stored with the posting, in a unit of measure Absent
P5 Determination engine Rules that return accounts, tax codes and dimension defaults from a context (transaction type, party, item class, branch, date and any registered attribute) Fixed roles plus party, bank and category defaults; the rule engine is proposed
P6 Allocation and distribution engine Moves cost or revenue between dimension values by any basis (fixed, revenue, quantity, statistical driver), with cycles and preview Planned for branch and cost centre only
P7 Accounting events A document raises an event; rules turn the event into balanced lines with a preview and re-derivation (subledger accounting) Absent: documents call the posting engine directly
P8 Runs Periodic processing with preview, approval, posting, period lock and audit Ad hoc (recurring postings); a general mechanism is planned in part (periods)
P9 Recognition schedules A schedule that releases an amount over time or by progress (deferred income, prepaid cost, depreciation, lease income, percentage of completion) Absent
P10 Currencies and parallel ledgers Transaction, functional and reporting currency; several ledgers for different accounting bases Absent
P11 Period model per module Calendar, status per module, adjustment periods, year-end Month lock; the rest planned
P12 Report value bases Amount, quantity, rate, budget, statistical; break-down by any dimension Amount, variance, percent; the rest proposed
P13 Extensible attributes Configurable fields on dimension values and on registered objects, without schema change Absent
P14 Lifecycle and workflow One state model for documents and objects with approval Approvals for four document types; other documents ad hoc

3. What is structural (decide now) and what is domain (decide when asked)

The primitives differ enormously in retrofit cost. Structural primitives change what a ledger line is, so they must be designed before more documents are built on the current shape; domain content can wait.

Group Primitives Why
Decide now (cheap today, very costly later) P3 typed references, P4 quantities, P10 currencies and ledgers, P7 accounting events, P9 recognition schedules They change the shape of a line, an entry or a document-to-ledger path
Decide soon (moderate) P1 account master, P2 dimension capabilities, P5 determination, P6 allocation, P13 attributes They are additions to existing tables and engines
Decide when a domain is asked for P8 runs (beyond the general mechanism), P11 per-module periods, P12 bases, P14 lifecycle, all domain modules They follow the structural choices

"Decide now" means decide the design, not necessarily build it: a design that reserves the shape (for example a nullable quantity and unit on a ledger line, a typed-reference table, an event layer in front of the posting engine) costs little and avoids a rewrite.

4. The domain extension contract

A domain module (retail, trade, manufacturing, real estate, contracting, services, fund accounting) is allowed to touch the core in six ways only:

  1. Register object types (for example work order, property, unit, lease, project, fund) with their lifecycle and attributes (P3, P13, P14).
  2. Register dimension types whose values mirror those objects (P2).
  3. Define accounting events and their line rules, with account roles and dimension and tax context (P7, P5).
  4. Seed determination rules and a chart and dimension template for the domain (P5, P1).
  5. Define schedules and runs (recognition, absorption, depreciation, allocation) (P9, P8, P6).
  6. Add permissions, screens, reports and translations in the usual way.

A domain module never adds ledger tables, a second posting path, a separate balance, or a report engine. Everything it posts goes through the one engine (AG-01) with its dimension tags (AG-10). This is the AG-11 wording the owner is asked to confirm.

5. Do the domains fit? Scenario by primitive

A mark means the scenario needs the primitive. Every scenario is expressible with the same fourteen primitives; none needs a primitive outside the list. (Illustrative, not exhaustive.)

Domain and scenario P1 P2 P3 P4 P5 P6 P7 P8 P9 P10 P12 P13
Retail: sales by store and channel, returns, tender clearing, gift cards, loyalty deferral x x x x x x x x x
Trading: import with landed cost and customs, letters of credit, consignment, commissions x x x x x x x x x
Manufacturing, order costing: cost per order, absorbed overhead, scrap, variances x x x x x x x x x x
Manufacturing, process costing: cost per process and period, equivalent units, joint and by-products x x x x x x x x
Manufacturing, standard costing: standards, variances, revaluation x x x x x x x x
Real estate development: land and development cost by phase and unit, off-plan sales over time, instalments, escrow, retention x x x x x x x x x x x
Real estate leasing: lease schedule, straight-lined rent, deposits, service-charge recovery by area, VAT by use x x x x x x x x x x x
Contracting and projects: percentage of completion, milestones, retention, advances, claims x x x x x x x x x x
Services: time and material, prepaid and deferred services, utilisation x x x x x x x x
Fund accounting (non-profit, waqf): balances per fund, restrictions x x x x x x
Multi-entity or multi-country: several books, currencies, taxes x x x x x x x

Reading: the most used primitives are P1, P2, P3, P5, P7; the ones the current core lacks that appear in almost every row are P3 (typed references), P4 (quantities), P7 (accounting events), P9 (recognition schedules), P10 (currencies and ledgers): that is the structural list in section 3.

6. Three illustrative traces (no domain table in the core)

Order costing (manufacturing). A work order is an object type with a dimension type of the same name. Material issue, traced labour and applied overhead are accounting events that debit work in progress with the work-order value as a tag; completion moves the cost to finished goods; scrap goes to a loss account. Cost per order is a ledger query by that tag; hours are quantities on the lines (P4); absorption is the allocation engine (P6) run as a run (P8). Core change needed: P3, P4, P7 only.

Off-plan unit sale (real estate development). A property, a phase and a unit are object types with dimension values. The customer's contract creates a receivable schedule and a contract liability; revenue is released over time by a recognition schedule keyed to the unit (P9) or at handover, per the accounting policy. Instalments settle through the normal receipt mechanism. Land and development cost sit in work in progress tagged by phase and are allocated to units by sellable area (a statistical driver, P4, P6). Escrow is a bank account flagged restricted (P1). Core change needed: P3, P4, P9, P1.

Rental of an investment property (real estate leasing). A lease is an object with a schedule of rent; invoices and straight-line income are schedule outputs (P9); the deposit is a liability tagged to the tenant and unit; service charges are recovered by area (P6); VAT depends on use (commercial standard-rated, residential exempt) through the item and unit tax class (P5). Core change needed: P3, P9, P5.

In all three the posting engine, the ledger lines, the report engine and the dimension framework are the same; only objects, events, rules and schedules differ.

7. Verdict on the current core

  • Sound and flexible: one engine, balanced entries, the unlimited dimension framework with rules per account, typed party tags, the report engine, the posting guard. These are the right foundations (AG-01, AG-10 to AG-16).
  • Not yet domain-neutral: a ledger line can only reference a customer or a vendor; it carries no quantity; documents post straight to the engine with no event layer; there is no schedule engine; there is one currency and one ledger. These five are the structural gaps (P3, P4, P7, P9, P10). They are small to reserve now and expensive to add after more documents and tables depend on the current shape.
  • Not a gap: work orders, leases, projects, units, funds and the like. They are domain modules to be built when asked, on the primitives above.

8. What not to do now

  • Do not build any domain module (work orders, projects, leases, units) without an explicit request.
  • Do not add domain-specific columns or tables to the ledger, the dimension tables or the posting engine.
  • Do not build the manufacturing, tax or real estate features proposed in other notes ahead of the structural primitives they depend on; the proposals in those notes are illustrations of what the structure must permit, not a build plan.

9. Decisions needed from the owner (structural only)

  1. Typed references (P3): one generic mechanism for referencing any registered object from a ledger line, replacing one column per subledger. Agree the direction?
  2. Quantities (P4): quantity and unit of measure on ledger lines, or a statistical ledger keyed by the same dimension combination?
  3. Accounting events (P7): introduce an event layer between documents and the posting engine (preview, re-derivation, rules per event, parallel ledgers later), or keep direct posting? This also underlies currency and tax determination.
  4. Recognition schedules (P9): one schedule engine for deferred income, prepaid cost, depreciation, lease income and progress-based recognition?
  5. Currency and ledgers (P10): decide the design now (functional and reporting currency, one or several ledgers), even if building is later.
  6. AG-11 wording: confirm that domain modules may own documents and lifecycle keyed by dimension values, while a second ledger or a hard-coded cost object is forbidden.
  7. Order: which of P3, P4, P7, P9, P10 is designed first (they interact; P7 and P10 first is the safest sequence).

10. Sources

  • Our code and register as of 2026-10-19; Finance Core Blueprint (docs/reference); the chart of accounts benchmark, the taxation analysis and the manufacturing and tax alignment note.
  • Tier-1 practice and IFRS: general knowledge, to be confirmed by the company's accountant.

Chapter 19

Does the architecture serve the core? Stress test against manufacturing cost accounting and multiple taxation

Source: docs/design-notes/manufacturing-and-tax-alignment.md · Stress test and proposal for decision: nothing built

Written 2026-10-19 at the owner's request: "align this with manufacturing domain cost accounting requirements, as well as multiple taxation requirements, to make sure the architecture serves the core."

Scope (owner, 2026-10-19): this note is not a request to build work orders or any manufacturing module. It tests whether the core structure could carry manufacturing cost accounting (including order costing) and multiple taxation if and when they are asked for. The proposals in sections 5 and 7 are illustrations of what the structure must permit, not a build plan. The structural conclusions are in the companion note A domain-neutral GL core.

Agreed entries that apply (register agreed-decisions-register): AG-01, AG-02, AG-04, AG-05 (follows); AG-10, AG-12, AG-13, AG-14, AG-15, AG-16 (follows); AG-11 (follows, with a tension, below); AG-17 (follows, widening needed); AG-30, AG-31, AG-32, AG-33 (follows); AG-71 and AG-77 (this note is the review they require). No agreed entry is contradicted.

The tension. The Blueprint (FR-WIP-01 to 08 and its entity list) describes work orders and projects as "cost objects" with their own tables and their own accumulated_* cost columns. AG-11 says management accounting is carried by the dimension framework and never by separate modules or ledgers. They are reconciled by the wording already proposed in the register (section J): a work order is a process document keyed by a dimension value; the ledger stays the single source of cost; the Blueprint's accumulated columns, if kept at all, are derived caches checked by the consistency check, never a second truth. This note assumes that wording. The owner must confirm it (decision 1).

Method. (1) State the architecture under test. (2) List what manufacturing cost accounting needs (tier-1 practice and IAS 2). (3) List what multiple taxation needs. (4) Walk one complete manufacturer through the architecture with numbers. (5) Record what holds, what is missing and the design adjustment. Tier-1 content is from general knowledge of SAP (product costing and controlling), Oracle (cost management), Dynamics 365 (production control and cost accounting) and IAS 2; clause-level details should be confirmed with the company's accountant.

1. The architecture under test

Layer Content Status
L0 Ledger One ledger, one posting engine, balanced entries, typed party tags, period guard Built
L1 Account master Classes, sub-classes, posting controls, tax category, currency, groups, templates, effective dating Proposed (chart benchmark 7.1)
L2 Dimension framework Unlimited types; roles (balancing, cost object, statistical driver); attributes; lifecycle; valid combinations; allocation over any type; break-down reports; budgets Types, values, combinations, rules, filters built; the rest proposed (7.3, 7.4)
L3 Determination One rule engine that returns tax codes and accounts from transaction type, party profile, item class, branch and date Proposed (tax design, chart benchmark 7.6)
L4 Process modules keyed by dimension Procurement and landed cost, production (work orders), projects, billing, budgets Procurement built (no landed cost); the rest planned or absent
L5 Reports One report engine with break-down, budget and quantity bases Engine built; bases proposed
L6 Periods Per-module status, runs with locks, year-end Month lock built; the rest planned (Sprints 12, 13)

2. What manufacturing cost accounting requires

# Requirement Tier-1 and standard practice
M1 Item classes and inventory accounts by class Item type (raw material, semi-finished, finished, trading goods, consumables, service) with a valuation class that decides the inventory, variance, price-difference and scrap accounts
M2 Costing method per item class Moving average, FIFO or standard cost; standard cost with variances; actual cost by order
M3 Bill of material, routing, work centres, activity types Materials, operations, labour and machine hours, with a rate per hour per cost centre and activity type
M4 Work order lifecycle Planned, released, issued, completed, closed; cost collected from material issues, traced labour, machine time, overhead application, subcontract charges
M5 Cost centre accounting and overhead absorption Pools of indirect cost, absorption by rate (labour hours, machine hours, units), allocation cycles between service and production cost centres, under- and over-absorption true-up
M6 Normal capacity and abnormal waste (IAS 2) Fixed overhead absorbed on normal capacity; abnormal waste and idle capacity expensed, not capitalised
M7 Work in progress Per-order cost to date; period-end WIP valuation (order costing) or equivalent units (process costing)
M8 Completion Transfer from WIP to finished goods at actual or standard cost per unit; goods receipt from production
M9 Variances Material price and usage, labour rate and efficiency, overhead spending, volume and capacity; posted and settled to cost of sales or inventory
M10 Scrap, rework, co-products and by-products Scrap to a loss account, by-product credit, joint-cost allocation by net realisable value or physical units
M11 Subcontracting (job work) Material sent to a vendor, stock held at the vendor, processing charge added to cost
M12 Valuation and write-down Lower of cost and net realisable value; revaluation; landed cost into raw material cost
M13 Statistical quantities Hours, units, floor area as drivers, stored with the posting
M14 Period runs and controls Overhead run, WIP run, variance settlement, cost roll-up; previewed, approved, period-locked, audited
M15 Reports Cost of goods manufactured, product cost per unit, order cost versus standard, variance analysis, WIP, inventory valuation by class, margin by product

3. What multiple taxation requires of a manufacturer

# Requirement
X1 Several rates and supply natures in one company (standard, zero-rated, exempt, out of scope) chosen from profiles, never by hand
X2 Recoverable and non-recoverable input tax; non-recoverable tax enters the cost of the item bought
X3 Import of raw material: customs value, duty (with exemptions for manufacturing inputs), import VAT, several payees, foreign currency
X4 Reverse charge and withholding on services bought from non-residents (engineering, licences, management fees)
X5 Exports and zero-rated sales with proof; exempt sales with recovery apportionment
X6 Excise on excisable goods, on release for consumption, included in the price and returned separately
X7 Deemed supplies (internal consumption, free samples, gifts of finished goods) with tax and no revenue
X8 Capital goods (machinery): input tax recovery and any adjustment rules
X9 Several tax registrations or jurisdictions in one company (a branch in another state), with tax on transfers between registrations
X10 Time of supply, advances from customers, credit and debit notes at the original rate
X11 Tax-exclusive standard cost; tax lines tagged with code, direction and return box; VAT return and registers from the ledger; settlement entry
X12 Effective-dated rates and rules; e-invoice category and exemption code from the same tax code

4. Worked example: one manufacturer through the architecture

A manufacturer buys local and imported raw material, runs one work order, scraps some units, sells domestically and exports. Amounts in riyals. Overhead and labour are assumed to carry no VAT, to keep the example short. Every line below is posted by the engine; the operator names only the party, the items and the work order. The bracket shows what the engine finds on its own.

Step Entry Found by the engine
1 Local raw material A: 1,000 + VAT 15% Dr Raw material 1,000; Dr VAT input 150; Cr AP (vendor) 1,150 Domestic purchase, recoverable (tax profile and item class); inventory account by valuation class
2 Import raw material B: goods 2,000, freight 50; customs value 2,050; duty 5% = 102.50; import VAT 15% on 2,152.50 = 322.88 Dr Raw material 2,152.50 (2,000 + 50 + 102.50 landed); Dr VAT input (import) 322.88; Cr AP supplier 2,000; Cr AP forwarder 50; Cr customs authority 425.38 (102.50 + 322.88) Foreign vendor and goods: import of goods; duty from the item's tariff rate; landed cost allocation; one payable per payee. Debits 2,475.38 = credits 2,475.38
3 Issue to work order WO-001: A 600, B 1,000 Dr WIP 1,600; Cr Raw material 1,600 Valuation class (raw material to WIP); tags: work order WO-001, cost centre Production
4 Traced direct labour 400 Dr WIP 400; Cr Direct labour absorbed 400 (the payroll expense is posted by payroll as an actual) Work order tag; labour is traced, not allocated
5 Overhead applied: 20 machine hours at 12.50 Dr WIP 250; Cr Overhead applied (clearing) 250 Absorption rule of the cost centre and activity type; statistical driver: 20 hours
6 Actual factory overhead of the month 500 Dr Factory overhead expense 500 (cost centre Production); Cr AP 500 An ordinary bill, tagged to the cost centre
7 Completion: 100 units made, 5 scrapped (abnormal) WIP total 2,250. Dr Inventory write-off 112.50; Cr WIP 112.50. Dr Finished goods 2,137.50; Cr WIP 2,137.50 (95 units at 22.50) Scrap rule; completion transfers actual cost to finished goods at the batch's unit cost
8 Period-end overhead true-up: actual 500, applied 250 Dr Overhead applied 250; Dr Cost of sales 250 (under-absorbed); Cr Factory overhead expense 500 Overhead run with preview and approval; nothing is lost or double-counted
9 Domestic sale 50 units at 40 + VAT 15% Dr AR 2,300; Cr Revenue 2,000; Cr VAT output 300. Dr Cost of sales 1,125; Cr Finished goods 1,125 Domestic standard-rated; tags: profit centre, product line, customer
10 Export 40 units at 38 Dr AR 1,520; Cr Revenue (export) 1,520; tax line of zero with zero-rated nature and reason code. Dr Cost of sales 900; Cr Finished goods 900 Foreign customer, export item: zero-rated; e-invoice category from the tax code
11 Filing the quarter Output 300; input 150 + 322.88 = 472.88; net refund 172.88. Dr VAT output 300; Dr VAT receivable from authority 172.88; Cr VAT input 472.88 Return from ledger tax tags; settlement entry

Result: finished goods left 5 units at 112.50; every cost reaches cost of sales or a visible loss; the ledger tags answer "cost of work order WO-001", "margin by product line", "overhead by cost centre" and "VAT by return box" from the same lines.

5. Findings: what holds, what is missing

5.1 What the architecture already supports

  • Cost collection by work order, cost centre and profit centre is the dimension framework: one combination per line, unlimited types, hierarchies, rules per account (a WIP account can require the work order type). No second ledger is needed (AG-10, AG-11, AG-12).
  • Tracing and absorption fit the allocation engine over any type (AG-17 widened): traced cost is posted straight to the work order; pooled cost is allocated by a rate or driver. This is the Blueprint's single "cost distribution" primitive.
  • Tax follows the same determination engine as accounts, so imported material, exports and non-recoverable tax cost resolve without operator input.
  • Reports: break-down by dimension and the variance columns already in the report engine cover work order, cost centre and product-line statements.

5.2 Gaps, in order of how much they block manufacturing

# Gap Design adjustment (proposal)
G1 One inventory account and one item class. Manufacturing needs raw material, WIP, finished goods and trading goods as separate reconcilable accounts, and price-difference, variance, scrap and overhead-applied accounts Item valuation class on the item master; roles per class (raw material, WIP, finished goods, variance, scrap, applied overhead, labour absorbed); the determination engine picks accounts by valuation class and movement type. Extends AG-02 and AG-04; no new module
G2 No work order, and no rule on how it relates to dimensions A work order is a document with a lifecycle whose creation also creates a dimension value of a system "work order" type (role: cost object). Every cost line carries it. Cost per work order is a ledger query. Any denormalised totals are caches under the consistency check
G3 Statistical quantities (hours, units) are not stored with postings Quantity and unit of measure on ledger lines (statistical key figures), or a statistical ledger of quantities keyed by the same dimension combination; required for rates, per-unit cost and capacity
G4 Costing method: average only Decide per item class: moving average for raw material; actual cost by order for work in progress and finished goods; standard cost with variances as an optional method. The variance accounts and the settlement run are part of the design from the start so that adding standard cost later is not a rewrite
G5 Overhead absorption rules Rate per cost centre and activity type and period, normal capacity per cost centre (IAS 2: fixed overhead on normal capacity, idle capacity and abnormal waste to expense), allocation cycles (service cost centres to production), preview, approval, true-up to cost of sales or variance
G6 Scrap, rework, co-products, by-products Basis types for the allocation engine: net realisable value and physical units; by-product credit account; scrap recovery sale as an ordinary sale with its tax class
G7 Subcontracting A document type and a stock at vendor location; the processing charge is a bill tagged to the work order; tax determination gets a transaction type for job work
G8 Period runs Overhead run, WIP valuation, variance settlement, cost roll-up as runs with preview, approval, period lock and audit, using the per-module period status of Sprint 12
G9 Imports and landed cost Foreign currency, landed cost sheets, customs entry (the tax programme, Sprint 14e) are prerequisites for imported raw material cost
G10 IAS 2 controls Net realisable value write-down run; rule that abnormal waste and unallocated fixed overhead are expensed; consistent cost formula per item class
G11 Reports Cost of goods manufactured, work order cost, variance analysis, WIP and inventory valuation by class need quantity and rate as value bases in the report engine beside amount and budget
G12 Standards and budgets Standard cost per product and period and budgets by cost centre and work order use the same dimension-combination structure as other budgets

5.3 Tax gaps specific to manufacturing

# Gap Design adjustment (proposal)
T1 Customs duty exemption for manufacturing inputs, drawback, bonded and free-zone inventory Item tax profile carries tariff code and duty rate; vendor and company profile carry exemption certificates with validity; determination returns duty zero when a valid certificate applies
T2 Non-recoverable tax must reach the cost of raw material and then the work order The tax engine posts the non-recoverable part to the inventory line (already designed); the cost then flows to work in progress through the issue, as in step 3
T3 Excise on excisable goods Excise component (Sprint 14f) with its own accounts, base rules and return; included in the selling price, not in manufacturing cost
T4 Deemed supplies (internal consumption, samples, gifts of finished goods) A transaction type "deemed supply" in the determination engine: tax output, no revenue, stock out at cost
T5 Capital goods (machinery) Tax category on the fixed-asset account (account master 7.1) plus recovery rules; adjustment rules to be confirmed with a tax adviser
T6 Several registrations or jurisdictions Tax registration on the branch (and company); determination takes the registration of the issuing branch; transfers between registrations get a transaction type; tax jurisdiction is an object in the tax design
T7 Time of supply and advances Tax point date on the document (date standard already allows it); advance receipts carry tax and are set off on the final invoice
T8 Scrap and by-product sales Their own item tax class through the same determination
T9 Withholding on services from non-residents (licences, engineering) Sprint 14d; vendor profile and service class decide the rate and treaty relief
T10 Standard cost must exclude recoverable tax Rule of the cost engine: costs are net of recoverable tax by construction, because only the non-recoverable part is posted to inventory

6. Verdict on the architecture, and effect on the agreed entries

  • It serves the core, with additions. The ledger, the dimension framework, the allocation primitive and the tax and account determination engine are the right foundation for manufacturing and multi-tax: the worked example resolved every step through them without a separate costing ledger.
  • What must be added to serve it: valuation classes and class roles (G1), the work order as a document keyed to a dimension value (G2), quantities with postings (G3), a costing-method decision and variance design (G4), absorption with capacity and cycles (G5), and the runs (G8). None changes an agreed entry; G1 and G2 depend on the AG-11 wording.
  • Review of agreed entries (register section J) updated by this test: AG-04 extends to valuation class and movement type; AG-05 gains a typed reference for the work order and the asset; AG-11 wording (process documents keyed by dimension values) is required by manufacturing, not optional; AG-12 reword stands; AG-17 widens to cycles, quantities and statistical drivers; AG-33 year-end is unaffected.
  • Sequencing consequence. Manufacturing sits on three prerequisites that are also needed by tax: account master and valuation classes, the determination engine, and foreign currency with landed cost. Building those first serves both domains; building manufacturing first would force rework.

7. What the structure must permit (illustrations, not a build plan, not registered)

Proposed id Content
BR-MFG-01 Item types and valuation classes; inventory, WIP, finished goods, variance, scrap and applied-overhead accounts by class through determination
BR-MFG-02 Work order document (planned, released, issued, completed, closed) creating a work-order dimension value; cost collection from issues, traced labour, subcontract charges
BR-MFG-03 Statistical quantities with postings (hours, units) and activity types per cost centre
BR-MFG-04 Overhead absorption: rates, normal capacity, allocation cycles, run with preview and approval, true-up
BR-MFG-05 Completion to finished goods at batch unit cost; scrap and rework; co-products and by-products
BR-MFG-06 Costing method per item class (average, actual by order, optional standard with variances); variance posting and settlement
BR-MFG-07 Subcontracting with stock at vendor and processing charge
BR-MFG-08 Period runs (overhead, WIP, variances, cost roll-up) with per-module period locks
BR-MFG-09 Net realisable value write-down and IAS 2 expense rules
BR-MFG-10 Manufacturing reports (cost of goods manufactured, work order cost, variance, WIP, valuation by class) with quantity and rate bases
BR-TAX-18 Transaction types for deemed supply, job work and transfers between registrations; tax registration per branch
BR-TAX-19 Duty exemption and drawback certificates on profiles with validity

8. Decisions needed from the owner

Only the structural decisions are needed now, and they are listed in the note A domain-neutral GL core (section 9): typed references, quantities on lines, an accounting-event layer, recognition schedules, currency and ledgers, and the AG-11 wording. The manufacturing choices (costing method, order versus process costing, capacity basis, subcontracting, several registrations) are decided when a manufacturing module is requested, not before.

9. Sources and confidence

  • Finance Core Blueprint (docs/reference): FR-WIP-01 to 08, FR-LC, FR-DIM, cost distribution rules, work orders and projects entities.
  • Our design notes: agreed-decisions register, chart of accounts benchmark, taxation analysis.
  • IAS 2 (inventories: cost of conversion, normal capacity, abnormal waste, net realisable value) and general tier-1 practice (SAP product costing and controlling, Oracle cost management, Dynamics 365 production and cost accounting), from general knowledge, not re-read in this session; confirm with the company's accountant before relying on specific clauses.
  • Saudi tax points in section 3 are marked in the taxation analysis as needing a tax adviser's confirmation.

Chapter 20

Chart of accounts: benchmark against tier-1 ERPs and fit to retail, trading, manufacturing, profit-centre, budgeting and project costing

Source: docs/design-notes/coa-tier1-benchmark.md · Benchmark and proposal for decision: nothing built

Written 2026-10-19 at the owner's request, after the owner observed that the finance core looked like "simple forms making entries". This is an analysis with a gap register and a target architecture for decision. Nothing is built or registered in the requirement set yet (section 9 proposes the entries).

Agreed entries that apply (register agreed-decisions-register): AG-02, AG-04, AG-05 and AG-06 (follows); AG-10 and AG-11 (follows after the correction below); AG-12 (follows, with a proposed wording change: it is marked Challenge in register section J); AG-14, AG-15, AG-17 (follows; widening proposed); AG-71 and AG-77 (this note is the review they require).

Correction on 2026-10-19 (owner): an earlier version of this note proposed separate profit centre and project objects. That contradicted the agreed design, in which dimensions are unlimited and flexible and serve budgeting, costing, control and profit and loss. Sections 4, 6, 7.3, 7.4 and 9 were rewritten so that every management-accounting need is met by capabilities of the dimension framework, not by new hard-coded modules.

Confidence. Our side (sections 2 and 5) was read from the code and the requirement register today. The tier-1 side is general product knowledge of SAP S/4HANA, Oracle Fusion General Ledger, Microsoft Dynamics 365 Finance and NetSuite, plus the documentation pages already checked for the tax analysis; account-attribute lists were not re-verified against current vendor documentation in this session and should be confirmed per vendor before a feature is copied from them.

1. What a chart of accounts is for, in a tier-1 system

A tier-1 chart is not a list of ledger accounts. It is the classification and control backbone of the whole finance system:

  1. It classifies every amount for statutory statements, management reporting, cash flow and tax (account type and sub-type, statement line, cash-flow class, nature versus function of expense).
  2. It controls posting: which documents may post to an account, whether it needs a currency, a tax category, a cost centre or a project, whether it is blocked, whether it is a reconciliation (control) account, whether line items are open items.
  3. It is the natural-account segment of a larger structure: with cost centres, profit centres, projects and other dimensions it forms the accounting combination that the ledger stores, and the system validates which combinations are allowed.
  4. It supports several views: operational chart, group (consolidation) chart, country or statutory chart, each mapped to the other.
  5. It is governed: changes are controlled, effective-dated, audited and cannot break history.
  6. It is the anchor of automation: account determination (documents find their own accounts) and templates per industry.

2. Our chart today (from the code)

Aspect What exists
Account record Code, English and Arabic name, one of five types (asset, liability, equity, revenue, expense), parent, heading flag, active flag, system role key, control flag, subledger type (customer, vendor, inventory, bank, tax, clearing, other), linked bank
Hierarchy Parent and child, headings that total, up to four levels, no loops, parent of the same type
Numbering Free text code, unique per company
Roles 16 system roles (receivable, payable, inventory, goods-received-not-invoiced, VAT input and output, VAT payable, vendor credit, revenue, cost of sales, direct operating expense, fixed assets, paid-in capital, retained earnings, cash rounding, bank) each mapped once per company
Control accounts Receivable, payable, inventory, banks, tax and clearing accounts are postable only by their own documents; a customer or vendor may name its own control account
Expense categories Each expense category maps to an account
Dimensions Configurable dimension types (branch and department seeded; any custom type), hierarchical values, rules per account (required, optional, not allowed, with default), default tags from document, party, bank and account, filters in all ledger reports
Starter chart One generic chart of about 35 accounts for every company; it includes accumulated depreciation, depreciation expense and zakat payable although there is no fixed-asset or zakat module
Governance Duplicate codes refused; type cannot change once postings exist; deactivation blocked with balance, role, control or children; Excel import with preview; no approval of chart changes; no effective dating
Statements A report designer builds statements from account selectors; the five types are the only built-in classification

3. How the tier-1 systems structure it

Concept SAP S/4HANA Oracle Fusion GL Dynamics 365 Finance NetSuite
Structure of the account Chart of accounts (operational, group, country) + G/L account with account group and company-code data; profit centre, segment, functional area, cost centre and project are standard fields of the universal journal Chart of accounts structure of segments (balancing, natural account, cost centre, intercompany, others), each with a value set and hierarchies Main account + financial dimensions, joined by account structures with rules for valid combinations Account + segments (department, class, location, custom) per subsidiary
Classification Account type (balance sheet, P&L; primary and secondary costs), financial statement version items, functional area, cash-flow relevance Account type per natural-account value (asset, liability, equity, revenue, expense); account hierarchies (trees) for reporting; cash-flow assignment Main account type and category (system categories drive reporting), posting type, financial reporting row definitions Account type list (bank, receivable, other current asset, fixed asset, payable, current liability, long-term liability, equity, income, cost of goods sold, expense, other) plus cash-flow rate type
Posting control Account group sets mandatory and hidden fields (field status); reconciliation account flag; open-item management; line-item display; tax category; blocked for posting Allow posting, allow budgeting, reconciliation flag at value level; cross-validation rules for combinations Debit or credit required, open items, account currency, default dimensions, dimension rules, blocked Restrict by department, class, location; subsidiary restriction; revenue and inventory options
Several views Operational, group and country charts with mapping; parallel ledgers (IFRS, local GAAP, tax) Several ledgers with ledger sets, secondary ledgers and reporting currencies Several ledgers and a consolidation account mapping Subsidiaries with elimination accounts
Profit centre accounting Profit centre and segment on every line; profit centre balance sheet; inter-profit-centre clearing Balancing segment gives balanced books per profit unit; intercompany balancing Financial dimension with balancing option and default dimensions Class or department as the profit unit
Cost objects Cost centres, internal orders, WBS elements, production orders Cost centre segment, project accounting (PPM) Project management and accounting with cost and revenue recognition Projects and jobs
Budget Budget and commitment (funds management, in public sector and enterprise), plan versions in controlling Budgetary control with funds checks, budget by segment combination and period Budget control (accounting-based), budget registers, cycles, plans Budgets by account, dimension and period
Currency Account currency, only-balances-in-local flag, revaluation key Account type of currency, revaluation and translation Account currency, revaluation, exchange-rate type Rate types per account
Templates Industry solutions and best-practice charts Seeded charts per localisation Localised charts SuiteSuccess industry charts
Governance Change documents, restricted fields once used, mass maintenance Value set security, effectivity dates on values Account structures with effective dates, inquiry of changes Role permissions, no deletion once used

The patterns that matter for us: classification attributes on the account, posting controls on the account, a dimension model with validation of combinations, several views of the same ledger, first-class profit centre, cost object and budget, effective-dated governance.

4. Benchmark: capability by capability

Status key: Built, Partial, Absent. "Retrofit cost" estimates how hard it is to add later without rewriting history (L low, M medium, H high).

# Capability Tier-1 standard Ours Status Retrofit
1 Type granularity Sub-types (current, non-current, cash, receivable ...), statement line, cash-flow class, nature versus function of expense Five types only; statements rely on selectors in the designer Partial L
2 Hierarchy Several hierarchies (statutory, management) over the same accounts One parent-child tree, four levels Partial M
3 Numbering and groups Account groups with number ranges and defaults Free code, no groups Absent L
4 Posting controls on the account Required fields, blocked, auto-post only, tax category, currency, open items Control flag and dimension rules; no block-for-posting distinct from inactive; no tax category; no currency Partial M
5 Reconciliation (control) accounts Per subledger, automatic Built, with per-party control accounts Built
6 Account currency and revaluation Per account Absent (no multi-currency) Absent H
7 Several charts or books Operational, group, statutory with mapping; parallel ledgers One chart per company, one ledger Absent H
8 Dimension model Segments or dimensions with valid-combination rules Dimensions with rules per account; no cross-validation between dimensions; no balancing dimension Partial M
9 Profit centre accounting Profit centre on every line, balanced books and balance sheet per profit centre, inter-unit clearing The dimension framework gives tags, hierarchy, rules per account and filters in every report. Missing in the framework: a balancing role, inter-unit clearing, break-down by value, per-value balance sheet Partial M
10 Cost objects Cost centres, internal orders, projects with WBS, production orders Any dimension type can serve as a cost object (cost centre is the department type). Missing in the framework: value attributes, lifecycle (status, validity dates), valid-combination rules; the allocation engine is planned for branch and cost centre only Partial M
11 Statistical accounts and units Quantity and statistical postings Absent Absent M
12 Budgeting Budget versions by account × dimensions × period; checks at posting or at commitment; variance reporting Absent; the Blueprint does not mention it. The framework fits it: budget lines can key on the existing dimension combination, and the report engine already has variance columns Absent M
13 Project accounting Project master, tasks or WBS, cost collection, billing, WIP, revenue recognition, retention A project can be a dimension type today (tag only). Missing: value attributes and lifecycle (framework), billing, work in progress, recognition and retention processes Partial H
14 Industry templates Charts for retail, trading, manufacturing, services, construction One generic chart of about 35 accounts Absent L
15 Manufacturing accounts Raw material, WIP, finished goods, variances, absorbed overhead One inventory control account; work in progress specified in the Blueprint, not built Absent H
16 Retail accounts Sales by channel, discounts and returns as contra-revenue, cash over and short, shrinkage, payment-provider clearing, gift card and loyalty liabilities Single sales revenue role; returns post through credit notes; no clearing or liability accounts for cards, vouchers, loyalty Partial M
17 Prepaid, accrued and deferred Prepaid expense, deferred revenue, accrued income, contract assets and liabilities, with release schedules Accrued expenses account and recurring accruals only Partial M
18 Provisions and write-offs Bad-debt provision, inventory obsolescence, write-off reasons Absent Absent M
19 Intercompany and elimination Intercompany accounts and elimination Absent Absent H
20 Year-end accounts Closing and carry-forward through retained earnings, with controls Opening balances built; year-end close planned (Sprint 13) Partial L
21 Statements Statement versions with comparatives, drill-down Report designer built Built
22 Localisation Standard Saudi chart aligned with the statutory format, bilingual Arabic names; chart is generic, not aligned with a standard format Partial L
23 Governance Effective dating, change approval, usage lock, change history Usage locks and audit; no effective dating or approval of chart changes Partial L
24 Import and mass maintenance Import with validation, mass change Excel import with preview Built

Reading the result. The mechanics (control accounts, tags, rules per account, unlimited dimensions, designer) are sound. The gaps that are expensive later are currency (6), several books (7), manufacturing accounts (15), intercompany (19), and the missing management-accounting capabilities of the dimension framework (9, 10, 12, 13). The cheap ones (classification, groups, templates, localisation, governance) should be done together as one enrichment of the account master.

5. Fit to business models

For each model: what the chart and dimensions must provide, what we provide, and the gap.

5.1 Retail

Need What a tier-1 retail setup has Ours
Revenue by channel and category Revenue accounts or dimensions per channel (store, online), category and brand One sales role; branch dimension; category not a dimension
Discounts, returns, allowances Contra-revenue accounts, so gross sales, discounts and returns show separately Discounts reduce the invoice; returns by credit note; no contra accounts
Payment tenders Cash, card, wallet and voucher clearing accounts reconciled to the provider settlement; provider fees Bank per tender through the bank account; no card-clearing and fee flow
Cash handling Cash over and short account, shift reconciliation, till float Shift close records variance; no posting account for it
Stock Inventory per warehouse and store, shrinkage, stock-take variance, transfer-in-transit Inventory control account; stock moves between warehouses and stock-take variances are recorded, but none of the 16 posting roles is an in-transit or shrinkage account
Liabilities Gift cards, vouchers, loyalty points, customer deposits None
Store profit Profit and loss per store, store balance sheet where used Branch tag on lines; branch profit and loss report; no per-store balance sheet

5.2 Trading (buy, import, sell)

Need Tier-1 setup Ours
Purchasing Goods-received-not-invoiced, price and quantity variances, purchase discounts, advances to suppliers Goods-received-not-invoiced built; no variance, discount or advance accounts
Imports Landed cost, customs, import VAT, foreign currency, exchange differences Absent (see the tax analysis)
Receivables risk Bad-debt provision, write-off, credit limits Absent
Margin Revenue and cost of sales by customer, product and branch Revenue and cost of sales by branch and department dimensions

5.3 Manufacturing

Need Tier-1 setup Ours
Inventory classes Raw material, work in progress, finished goods, each reconcilable One inventory account
Production accounting Production orders collect material, labour and overhead; variances (price, usage, efficiency, overhead) Absent (no production module)
Overhead Absorbed or applied overhead account, cost centre allocation Allocation planned (Sprint 15), absorption absent
Standard versus actual costing Costing method per item class Average only
Scrap, by-products, subcontracting Dedicated accounts and flows Absent

5.4 Profit-centre accounting

Tier-1 pattern: every line carries a profit centre (and, in SAP, a segment); balance sheet and profit and loss can be produced per profit centre; transfers between profit centres clear through inter-profit-centre accounts; shared costs are allocated by rule. Ours: a profit centre is simply a dimension type (branch or any custom type) with tags, hierarchy and report filters, and an allocation run is planned. Missing in the dimension framework (section 7.3): a balancing role, inter-unit clearing, value attributes (manager, status), break-down reports per value, allocation over any type.

5.5 Budgeting

Tier-1 pattern: budget versions (original, revised, forecast) by account (or account group) × dimensions × period; control at posting or at purchase commitment (warn or stop); commitments from requisitions and orders; variance reports (actual versus budget versus prior year). Ours: nothing built, but the dimension combination and the report engine's variance columns are the right base: a budget line keys on an account (or group) and a dimension combination, and budget becomes a value basis of the designer (section 7.4). Account classification (items 1 and 3) and a commitment concept are the missing prerequisites.

5.6 Project-level costing

Tier-1 pattern: a project master (customer, dates, manager, status, contract value, budget) with tasks or WBS; costs collected from purchases, expenses, timesheets, inventory issues; billing (fixed price, milestone, time and material); work in progress and revenue recognition (percentage of completion, completed contract); retention; project profitability. Ours: a custom dimension type can tag lines with a project, which is the right key. Missing: value attributes and lifecycle on dimension values, dimensions on documents (Sprint 16), and the processes (billing, work in progress, recognition, retention), which are documents and runs keyed by the project value, not a separate ledger.

6. Findings

Structurally sound (keep): company-owned chart with hierarchy; control accounts and per-party control accounts; typed subledger tags; dimensions with rules per account and default tags; the report designer; usage locks.

Thin, in order of how much future development they block:

  1. Account master has too few attributes. With five types and a control flag only, nothing can be classified for cash flow, statutory lines, current versus non-current, nature versus function, tax category, currency or blocked status. Every later feature (tax, currency, cash-flow statement, budgeting by account group) will want these. Low retrofit cost now, high if each feature adds its own.
  2. The dimension framework lacks the capabilities that make a dimension useful for management accounting. Dimensions are already unlimited and flexible (any number of types, hierarchical values, rules per account, filters in reports). What a type cannot yet do: carry attributes and a lifecycle, be a balancing unit, validate combinations with other types, break a report down by its values, be a basis of allocation beyond branch and cost centre, or key a budget. These are additions to the framework, not new modules (section 7.3).
  3. One chart, one ledger, one currency. Foreign currency and several books are structural and must be decided before more documents are built on single-currency assumptions.
  4. No account groups and no industry templates. Every company starts from the same generic chart; a retailer, a trader and a manufacturer need different starting structures and different automatic accounts.
  5. Role-based account lookup is flat. One account per role per company. Tier-1 systems determine accounts by role and by item or product class, party class, branch and transaction type (SAP account determination, Oracle subledger accounting rules). This is the same point as the tax analysis: the document should name what and who, and rules find the accounts.
  6. Governance. No effective dating or approval of chart changes; acceptable for small companies, not for audited ones.

7. Target architecture (one design, for decision)

7.1 Enrich the account master (one slice, low cost, unblocks everything)

Attribute Purpose
Account class and sub-class Cash, bank, receivable, other current asset, non-current asset, current or non-current liability, equity, revenue (operating, other), cost of sales, operating expense, finance cost, tax, zakat; drives statutory and management statements
Expense nature or function By-nature and by-function presentations of the same expense
Cash-flow class Operating, investing, financing, or non-cash, for the cash flow statement
Statement mapping key Link to statutory statement lines (per chart view)
Posting control Blocked for posting (distinct from inactive), automatic posting only, manual journal allowed, requires dimension (already), requires currency, statistical
Tax category The tax treatment of postings to the account (for example outside VAT scope), for the tax engine
Account group Defaults and number ranges; chosen at creation
Currency Functional only, or fixed foreign currency, or any (when currency exists)
Effective from and to Lifecycle without deleting history

7.2 Chart views and templates

  • Chart templates per industry (retail, trading, manufacturing, services and projects) with their automatic accounts and dimension rules, reviewed by an accountant; a company picks one at creation and can adopt a template later by merge.
  • Chart views: statutory (local standard and IFRS), management, group. A view is a mapping from accounts to the view's lines, so one ledger serves several statements; true parallel ledgers are a later decision (7.5).

7.3 Management accounting through the dimension framework (no separate modules)

Principle, as agreed and confirmed against the code: dimensions are unlimited and configurable. A company can define any number of dimension types; a ledger line carries one tag per type through a dimension combination; values form hierarchies; rules per account make a type required, optional or not allowed; every report row and column can filter by any type. Profit centre, cost centre, project, product line, region and channel are all dimension types, not special modules. Nothing in section 7 may add a hard-coded "profit centre module" or "project module" ledger.

The benchmark gap is therefore narrower and more precise than "no profit centre object": today a dimension type has tags, hierarchy and rules, and nothing else. The capabilities below are added to the framework itself, so that any type can use them.

# Capability added to the dimension framework What it gives Typical use
1 Type roles (flags on the type): balancing, cost object, statistical driver Tells the engine how to treat the type Profit centre = balancing; project and cost centre = cost object; headcount or floor area = statistical driver
2 Value attributes: configurable fields per type (text, number, date, status, link to customer, employee or branch) A dimension value becomes real master data Project: customer, dates, manager, contract value. Profit centre: manager, responsible branch
3 Value lifecycle: status and validity dates; posting refused outside them Closing a project or cost centre is enforced Project completed, cost centre retired
4 Valid-combination rules between types: requires, implies, default derivation Keeps combinations meaningful A project belongs to one profit centre, so choosing the project sets it
5 Balancing: for a balancing type, each journal entry nets to zero per value; the engine adds inter-unit clearing lines when it does not; balance sheet and profit and loss per value Profit centre accounting without a separate ledger Store or division books that balance, with transfers cleared through an inter-unit account
6 Break-down reports: a column or row per value of a chosen type, rolled up through the hierarchy, drill to the ledger Profit and loss, balance sheet or cost report by any dimension in one run, no hand-built columns Profit and loss per branch, per project, per product line
7 Allocation engine over any type: a rule moves cost from source values and accounts to destination values by a basis (fixed percentage, revenue, a statistical driver, another column), net zero Overhead absorption and cost distribution Head office cost to branches; shared service cost to projects
8 Dimensions on every document (Sprint 16) with Required rules enforced Costing from sales, purchases and expenses without a journal A purchase bill requires a project on construction material accounts
9 Rules per account do the project accounting work: work in progress, unbilled revenue, deferred revenue and retention accounts mark the project type as required A project's balances are queries over the ledger, not a second ledger Project profitability, work in progress per project

Project billing (fixed price, milestone, time and material) and revenue recognition (percentage of completion, completed contract) are processes: documents and runs keyed by the project dimension value, posting through the normal engine. They are built after items 2, 3 and 8, and need the accounting-rules layer (the same as for tax). They are not a separate project ledger.

7.4 Budgeting on the same framework

  • Budget version (original, revised, forecast) with lines of: account or account group × dimension combination (any subset of the company's types, stored in the existing combination structure) × fiscal period × amount, with an optional quantity.
  • Controls: a company chooses which dimension types a control applies to (for example department plus account group, or project plus all cost accounts), and the mode (report only, warn, stop), at posting, requisition and order.
  • Commitments: open requisitions and orders carry the same dimension combination and count against the budget until invoiced.
  • Reporting: budget is a value basis of the report designer (columns today are period basis × value basis × dimension filter, with variance and percent columns already built), so budget versus actual versus prior year works for any dimension without new report code.
  • Consequence: there is no limit on dimensions for budgeting, costing or control; the limit is only what the company configures.

7.5 Foundations to decide first

Foreign currency (account currency, rates, revaluation) and parallel ledgers are not features to add to the chart later; they change what an amount is. They must be decided (even if built later) before profit centre, project and budget design is frozen.

7.6 Account determination

Documents name party, item, branch and transaction type; determination rules (the same mechanism as the tax design) return the accounts, so revenue, discount, return, cost of sales, inventory, variance and clearing accounts follow the item class and the profile rather than one account per role.

8. How the pieces fit with the tax design

The tax design (Sprints 14 to 14f) needs exactly the account attributes in 7.1 (tax category, currency, posting control) and the determination mechanism in 7.6. They should be one engine, one sprint line, not two.

9. Proposed requirements, tests and sprints (not yet registered)

10. Decisions needed from the owner

  1. Order: account-master enrichment and templates first (cheap, unblocks tax, currency and budgeting), then the dimension-framework capabilities (roles, attributes, balancing, break-down reports, allocation over any type) and budgeting, or the other way round?
  2. Multi-currency and parallel ledgers: in the programme, and before the dimension-framework capabilities are frozen?
  3. Which business models are real customers' needs now: retail, trading, manufacturing, projects? The templates and the first accounts follow that.
  4. Profit centre versus branch: is the branch dimension type the balancing (profit-centre) type, or is profit centre a separate dimension type across branches? Either is possible in the framework; the answer sets the default company setup.
  5. Project costing depth: cost collection and profitability only, or billing and revenue recognition (percentage of completion) as well?
  6. Budget control: report only, warn, or stop at posting and at purchase commitment?
  7. Statutory view: which standard (local, IFRS, both) must the chart map to, and who signs off the template charts?
